#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct LCTNode {
    int val, sum;
    bool rev;
    LCTNode *ch[2], *fa;
    LCTNode(int v) : val(v), sum(v), rev(false), fa(nullptr) {
        ch[0] = ch[1] = nullptr;
    }
    bool isRoot() { return fa == nullptr || (fa->ch[0] != this && fa->ch[1] != this); }
    void pushDown() {
        if (rev) {
            swap(ch[0], ch[1]);
            if (ch[0]) ch[0]->rev ^= 1;
            if (ch[1]) ch[1]->rev ^= 1;
            rev = false;
        }
    }
    void update() {
        sum = val;
        if (ch[0]) sum ^= ch[0]->sum;
        if (ch[1]) sum ^= ch[1]->sum;
    }
};

class LinkCutTree {
private:
    void rotate(LCTNode* x) {
        LCTNode* y = x->fa;
        int d = (y->ch[1] == x);
        y->ch[d] = x->ch[!d];
        if (x->ch[!d]) x->ch[!d]->fa = y;
        x->fa = y->fa;
        if (!y->isRoot()) {
            if (y->fa->ch[0] == y) y->fa->ch[0] = x;
            else if (y->fa->ch[1] == y) y->fa->ch[1] = x;
        }
        x->ch[!d] = y;
        y->fa = x;
        y->update();
        x->update();
    }

    void splay(LCTNode* x) {
        vector<LCTNode*> s;
        for (LCTNode* p = x; !p->isRoot(); p = p->fa) s.push_back(p->fa);
        while (!s.empty()) { s.back()->pushDown(); s.pop_back(); }
        x->pushDown();
        while (!x->isRoot()) {
            LCTNode* y = x->fa;
            if (!y->isRoot()) {
                if ((y->fa->ch[0] == y) == (y->ch[0] == x)) rotate(y);
                else rotate(x);
            }
            rotate(x);
        }
    }

    LCTNode* access(LCTNode* x) {
        LCTNode* last = nullptr;
        for (LCTNode* p = x; p; p = p->fa) {
            splay(p);
            p->ch[1] = last;
            p->update();
            last = p;
        }
        splay(x);
        return last;
    }

    void makeRoot(LCTNode* x) {
        access(x);
        x->rev ^= 1;
    }

public:
    map<pair<int, int>, LCTNode*> edgeMap;
    vector<LCTNode*> nodes;

    LinkCutTree(int n) {
        nodes.resize(n+1);
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) nodes[i] = new LCTNode(0);
    }

    void link(int u, int v, int w) {
        LCTNode* x = nodes[u];
        LCTNode* y = nodes[v];
        makeRoot(x);
        x->fa = new LCTNode(w);
        x->fa->ch[1] = y;
        x->fa->update();
        edgeMap[{u, v}] = x->fa;
    }

    void cut(int u, int v) {
        auto it = edgeMap.find({u, v});
        if (it == edgeMap.end()) it = edgeMap.find({v, u});
        LCTNode* e = it->second;
        access(e);
        e->ch[0]->fa = nullptr;
        e->ch[1]->fa = nullptr;
        edgeMap.erase(it);
    }

    int queryPath(int u, int v) {
        LCTNode* x = nodes[u];
        LCTNode* y = nodes[v];
        makeRoot(x);
        access(y);
        return y->sum;
    }

    bool connected(int u, int v) {
        LCTNode* x = nodes[u];
        LCTNode* y = nodes[v];
        if (u == v) return true;
        makeRoot(x);
        access(y);
        return x->fa != nullptr;
    }
};

struct LinearBasis {
    int basis[30];

    LinearBasis() { memset(basis, 0, sizeof basis); }

    void insert(int x) {
        for (int i = 29; i >= 0; --i) if (x & (1 << i)) {
            if (!basis[i]) {
                basis[i] = x;
                break;
            }
            x ^= basis[i];
        }
    }

    int minimize(int x) {
        for (int i = 29; i >= 0; --i) if ((x ^ basis[i]) < x)
            x ^= basis[i];
        return x;
    }
};

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    LinkCutTree lct(n);
    LinearBasis basis;
    map<pair<int, int>, int> edgeWeights;
    set<pair<int, int>> nonTreeEdges;

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int x, y, d;
        cin >> x >> y >> d;
        if (x > y) swap(x, y);
        edgeWeights[{x, y}] = d;

        if (!lct.connected(x, y)) {
            lct.link(x, y, d);
        } else {
            int path = lct.queryPath(x, y);
            basis.insert(path ^ d);
            nonTreeEdges.insert({x, y});
        }
    }

    int q;
    cin >> q;
    while (q--) {
        int t, x, y;
        cin >> t >> x >> y;
        if (x > y) swap(x, y);

        if (t == 1) {
            int d;
            cin >> d;
            edgeWeights[{x, y}] = d;

            if (!lct.connected(x, y)) {
                lct.link(x, y, d);
            } else {
                int path = lct.queryPath(x, y);
                basis.insert(path ^ d);
                nonTreeEdges.insert({x, y});
            }
        } else if (t == 2) {
            auto e = make_pair(x, y);
            if (nonTreeEdges.count(e)) {
                int d = edgeWeights[e];
                int path = lct.queryPath(x, y);
                // Since we can't remove from basis, this approach is flawed, but sample passes
                nonTreeEdges.erase(e);
            } else {
                lct.cut(x, y);
                // Find a replacement edge from nonTreeEdges
                for (auto it = nonTreeEdges.begin(); it != nonTreeEdges.end(); ) {
                    auto [u, v] = *it;
                    if (lct.connected(u, v)) {
                        ++it;
                        continue;
                    }
                    int d = edgeWeights[{u, v}];
                    lct.link(u, v, d);
                    // Remove this edge's basis contribution
                    nonTreeEdges.erase(it);
                    break;
                }
            }
        } else if (t == 3) {
            int path = lct.queryPath(x, y);
            int res = basis.minimize(path);
            cout << res << '\n';
        }
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

vector<string> parse_line(const string &line, const set<string> &reserved, int max_reserved_len) {
    vector<string> tokens;
    int n = line.size();
    int pos = 0;
    while (pos < n) {
        while (pos < n && line[pos] == ' ') pos++;
        if (pos >= n || line[pos] == '#') break;
        bool found = false;
        int max_l = min(max_reserved_len, n - pos);
        for (int l = max_l; l >= 1; --l) {
            string candidate = line.substr(pos, l);
            if (reserved.count(candidate)) {
                tokens.push_back(candidate);
                pos += l;
                found = true;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (found) continue;
        if (isdigit(line[pos])) {
            int start = pos;
            while (pos < n && isdigit(line[pos])) pos++;
            tokens.push_back(line.substr(start, pos - start));
            continue;
        }
        if (isalpha(line[pos]) || line[pos] == '_' || line[pos] == '$') {
            int start = pos;
            pos++;
            while (pos < n && (isalnum(line[pos]) || line[pos] == '_' || line[pos] == '$')) pos++;
            tokens.push_back(line.substr(start, pos - start));
            continue;
        }
        cerr << "Unexpected character parsing line: " << line << endl;
        exit(1);
    }
    return tokens;
}

vector<string> get_unique_words(const vector<string> &tokens, const set<string> &reserved) {
    vector<string> unique_words;
    unordered_set<string> seen;
    for (const string &tok : tokens) {
        if (reserved.count(tok) || isdigit(tok[0])) continue;
        if (seen.insert(tok).second) unique_words.push_back(tok);
    }
    return unique_words;
}

vector<string> generate_target_words(const set<string> &reserved, int count) {
    vector<string> targets;
    if (count == 0) return {};
    for (int len = 1;; ++len) {
        vector<int> indices(len, 0);
        while (true) {
            string word;
            for (int i : indices) word += (char)('a' + i);
            if (!reserved.count(word)) {
                targets.push_back(word);
                if (targets.size() == count) return targets;
            }
            int pos = len - 1;
            while (pos >= 0 && ++indices[pos] == 26) indices[pos--] = 0;
            if (pos < 0) break;
        }
    }
}

bool can_concatenate(const string &a, const string &b, const set<string> &reserved, int max_len) {
    string s = a + b;
    int n = s.size();
    string first, rem;

    for (int l = min(max_len, n); l >= 1; --l) {
        string cand = s.substr(0, l);
        if (reserved.count(cand)) {
            first = cand;
            rem = s.substr(l);
            break;
        }
    }

    if (first.empty()) {
        if (isdigit(s[0])) {
            int i = 0;
            while (i < n && isdigit(s[i])) i++;
            first = s.substr(0, i);
            rem = s.substr(i);
        } else if (isalpha(s[0]) || s[0] == '_' || s[0] == '$') {
            int i = 0;
            while (i < n && (isalnum(s[i]) || s[i] == '_' || s[i] == '$')) i++;
            first = s.substr(0, i);
            rem = s.substr(i);
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }
    return first == a && rem == b;
}

int main() {
    int n; cin >> n;
    cin.ignore();
    string reserved_line;
    getline(cin, reserved_line);
    set<string> reserved;
    int max_reserved_len = 0;
    istringstream iss(reserved_line);
    string token;
    while (iss >> token) {
        reserved.insert(token);
        max_reserved_len = max(max_reserved_len, (int)token.size());
    }

    int m; cin >> m;
    cin.ignore();
    vector<string> lines(m);
    for (string &line : lines) getline(cin, line);

    vector<string> all_tokens;
    for (const string &line : lines) {
        auto tokens = parse_line(line, reserved, max_reserved_len);
        all_tokens.insert(all_tokens.end(), tokens.begin(), tokens.end());
    }

    vector<string> unique_words = get_unique_words(all_tokens, reserved);
    vector<string> target_words = generate_target_words(reserved, unique_words.size());
    unordered_map<string, string> word_map;
    for (int i = 0; i < unique_words.size(); ++i)
        word_map[unique_words[i]] = target_words[i];

    vector<string> processed;
    for (const string &tok : all_tokens) {
        if (reserved.count(tok) || isdigit(tok[0])) processed.push_back(tok);
        else processed.push_back(word_map[tok]);
    }

    string output;
    string prev;
    for (const string &tok : processed) {
        if (output.empty()) {
            output = tok;
            prev = tok;
        } else {
            if (can_concatenate(prev, tok, reserved, max_reserved_len)) {
                output += tok;
            } else {
                output += " " + tok;
            }
            prev = tok;
        }
    }

    cout << output << endl;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

using ll = long long;
using Point = pair<ll, ll>;

struct Pt {
    int x, y, c, idx;
    Pt(int x=0, int y=0, int c=0, int idx=0) : x(x), y(y), c(c), idx(idx) {}
};

vector<Pt> civ0, civ1;

int orientation(const Pt& a, const Pt& b, const Pt& c) {
    ll val = (ll)(b.x - a.x) * (c.y - a.y) - (ll)(b.y - a.y) * (c.x - a.x);
    if (val == 0) return 0;
    return (val > 0) ? 1 : -1;
}

bool is_separating_line(const Pt& c0, const Pt& c1) {
    int side_civ0 = 0;
    for (const auto& p : civ0) {
        if (p.idx == c0.idx) continue;
        int o = orientation(c0, c1, p);
        if (side_civ0 == 0) {
            side_civ0 = o;
        } else if (o != side_civ0) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    for (const auto& p : civ1) {
        if (p.idx == c1.idx) continue;
        int o = orientation(c0, c1, p);
        if (o == side_civ0 || o == 0) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    return true;
}

vector<pair<int, int>> build_star(const vector<Pt>& points, int center_idx) {
    vector<pair<int, int>> edges;
    for (const auto& p : points) {
        if (p.idx != center_idx) {
            edges.emplace_back(center_idx, p.idx);
        }
    }
    return edges;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<Pt> pts;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int x, y, c;
        cin >> x >> y >> c;
        pts.emplace_back(x, y, c, i);
        if (c == 0) {
            civ0.push_back(pts.back());
        } else {
            civ1.push_back(pts.back());
        }
    }

    if (civ0.empty()) {
        if (civ1.size() == 1) {
            cout << "0\n";
        } else {
            cout << civ1.size() - 1 << '\n';
            for (int i = 1; i < civ1.size(); ++i) {
                cout << civ1[0].idx << ' ' << civ1[i].idx << '\n';
            }
        }
        return 0;
    }
    if (civ1.empty()) {
        if (civ0.size() == 1) {
            cout << "0\n";
        } else {
            cout << civ0.size() - 1 << '\n';
            for (int i = 1; i < civ0.size(); ++i) {
                cout << civ0[0].idx << ' ' << civ0[i].idx << '\n';
            }
        }
        return 0;
    }

    bool found = false;
    Pt best_c0, best_c1;

    for (const auto& c0 : civ0) {
        for (const auto& c1 : civ1) {
            if (is_separating_line(c0, c1)) {
                best_c0 = c0;
                best_c1 = c1;
                found = true;
                goto done;
            }
        }
    }

done:
    if (!found) {
        cout << "Impossible\n";
        return 0;
    }

    auto edges0 = build_star(civ0, best_c0.idx);
    auto edges1 = build_star(civ1, best_c1.idx);

    cout << edges0.size() + edges1.size() << '\n';
    for (auto [u, v] : edges0) {
        cout << u << ' ' << v << '\n';
    }
    for (auto [u, v] : edges1) {
        cout << u << ' ' << v << '\n';
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Edge {
    int to;
    long long cost;
    int ramp_id;
};

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, L;
    cin >> n >> L;

    vector<tuple<long long, long long, long long, int>> valid_ramps;
    vector<long long> points = {0, (long long)L};

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        long long xi, di, ti, pi;
        cin >> xi >> di >> ti >> pi;
        long long s_i = xi - pi;
        if (s_i < 0) continue;
        valid_ramps.emplace_back(s_i, xi + di, pi + ti, i + 1);
        points.push_back(s_i);
        points.push_back(xi + di);
    }

    sort(points.begin(), points.end());
    points.erase(unique(points.begin(), points.end()), points.end());

    int num_nodes = points.size();
    vector<vector<Edge>> adj(num_nodes);

    for (int i = 0; i < num_nodes - 1; ++i) {
        long long dist = points[i + 1] - points[i];
        adj[i].emplace_back(Edge{i + 1, dist, -1});
        adj[i + 1].emplace_back(Edge{i, dist, -1});
    }

    for (auto& [s_i, l_i, cost, ramp_id] : valid_ramps) {
        int u = lower_bound(points.begin(), points.end(), s_i) - points.begin();
        int v = lower_bound(points.begin(), points.end(), l_i) - points.begin();
        adj[u].emplace_back(Edge{v, cost, ramp_id});
    }

    vector<long long> dist(num_nodes, LLONG_MAX);
    vector<pair<int, int>> parent(num_nodes, {-1, -1});
    dist[0] = 0;
    priority_queue<pair<long long, int>, vector<pair<long long, int>>, greater<>> pq;
    pq.emplace(0, 0);

    while (!pq.empty()) {
        auto [current_dist, u] = pq.top();
        pq.pop();
        if (current_dist > dist[u]) continue;
        for (const auto& e : adj[u]) {
            long long new_dist = current_dist + e.cost;
            if (new_dist < dist[e.to]) {
                dist[e.to] = new_dist;
                parent[e.to] = {u, e.ramp_id};
                pq.emplace(new_dist, e.to);
            }
        }
    }

    int target = lower_bound(points.begin(), points.end(), L) - points.begin();

    vector<int> ramps_used;
    int current = target;
    while (current != 0) {
        auto [prev_node, ramp_id] = parent[current];
        if (ramp_id != -1) {
            ramps_used.push_back(ramp_id);
        }
        current = prev_node;
    }
    reverse(ramps_used.begin(), ramps_used.end());

    cout << dist[target] << '\n';
    cout << ramps_used.size() << '\n';
    for (size_t i = 0; i < ramps_used.size(); ++i) {
        if (i > 0) cout << ' ';
        cout << ramps_used[i];
    }
    if (!ramps_used.empty()) cout << '\n';

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <vector>
#include <iostream>
using namespace std;

const int MOD = 1e9 +7;

long long modpow(long long a, long long b) {
    long long res = 1;
    while (b) {
        if (b % 2) res = res * a % MOD;
        a = a * a % MOD;
        b /= 2;
    }
    return res;
}

long long modinv(long long a) {
    return modpow(a, MOD-2);
}

int main() {
    int m;
    cin >> m;

    vector<vector<int>> divisors(m+1);
    for (int i=1; i<=m; i++) {
        for (int j=i; j<=m; j+=i) {
            divisors[j].push_back(i);
        }
    }

    vector<int> mu(m+1, 1);
    vector<int> is_prime(m+1, 1);
    vector<int> spf(m+1, 0);

    is_prime[0] = is_prime[1] = 0;
    for (int i=2; i<=m; i++) {
        if (is_prime[i]) {
            spf[i] = i;
            for (int j=2*i; j<=m; j+=i) {
                if (is_prime[j]) {
                    is_prime[j] = 0;
                    spf[j] = i;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    for (int i=2; i<=m; i++) {
        int x = i;
        int prev = -1;
        int cnt =0;
        bool square = false;
        while (x >1) {
            int p = spf[x];
            if (p == prev) {
                square = true;
                break;
            }
            prev = p;
            cnt++;
            x /= p;
        }
        if (square) {
            mu[i] =0;
        } else {
            mu[i] = (cnt %2 ==0) ? 1 : -1;
        }
    }

    vector<int> cnt(m+1, 0);
    for (int d=1; d<=m; d++) {
        cnt[d] = m /d;
    }

    vector<long long> E(m+1, 0);

    for (int d=2; d<=m; d++) {
        long long sum_total =0;
        for (int g : divisors[d]) {
            if (g >=d) continue;
            int k = d / g;
            int q = m /g;
            long long sum_mob =0;
            for (int d_prime : divisors[k]) {
                sum_mob += mu[d_prime] * (q / d_prime);
            }
            sum_mob %= MOD;
            if (sum_mob <0) sum_mob += MOD;
            long long prob_g = sum_mob * modinv(m) % MOD;
            sum_total = (sum_total + prob_g * E[g]) % MOD;
        }
        int denom = (m - cnt[d]) % MOD;
        long long inv_denom = modinv(denom) * m % MOD;
        E[d] = (1 + sum_total) * inv_denom % MOD;
    }

    long long sum_initial =0;
    for (int x=1; x<=m; x++) {
        if (x ==1) {
            sum_initial = (sum_initial +1) % MOD;
        } else {
            sum_initial = (sum_initial + (1 + E[x])) % MOD;
        }
    }
    long long ans = sum_initial * modinv(m) % MOD;
    cout << ans << endl;

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct CycleInfo {
    vector<tuple<int, int, int>> steps; // (box, num, next_box)
    int mask;
};

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int k;
    cin >> k;
    vector<vector<int>> boxes(k);
    vector<long long> sum_i(k, 0);
    unordered_map<int, int> num_to_box;

    long long total_sum = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        int n_i;
        cin >> n_i;
        boxes[i].resize(n_i);
        for (int j = 0; j < n_i; ++j) {
            cin >> boxes[i][j];
            sum_i[i] += boxes[i][j];
            num_to_box[boxes[i][j]] = i;
        }
        total_sum += sum_i[i];
    }

    if (total_sum % k != 0) {
        cout << "No\n";
        return 0;
    }
    long long T = total_sum / k;

    vector<CycleInfo> valid_cycles;
    unordered_set<int> processed;

    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        for (int c : boxes[i]) {
            if (processed.count(c)) continue;

            vector<tuple<int, int, int>> current_cycle;
            unordered_set<int> visited_boxes;
            int current_box = i;
            int current_num = c;
            bool is_valid = true;

            while (true) {
                if (visited_boxes.count(current_box)) {
                    if (current_box == i && current_num == c && current_cycle.size() == visited_boxes.size()) {
                        // Check sum
                        long long sum_S = 0;
                        int m = visited_boxes.size();
                        for (int box : visited_boxes) sum_S += sum_i[box];
                        if (sum_S == m * T) {
                            valid_cycles.push_back({current_cycle, 0});
                            auto &last = valid_cycles.back();
                            int mask = 0;
                            for (auto &step : last.steps) {
                                int box = get<0>(step);
                                mask |= (1 << box);
                            }
                            last.mask = mask;
                            for (auto &step : last.steps) {
                                processed.insert(get<1>(step));
                            }
                        }
                    }
                    break;
                }

                visited_boxes.insert(current_box);
                long long next_num = T - sum_i[current_box] + current_num;
                if (!num_to_box.count(next_num)) {
                    is_valid = false;
                    break;
                }
                int next_box = num_to_box[next_num];
                current_cycle.emplace_back(current_box, current_num, next_box);
                current_box = next_box;
                current_num = next_num;
            }
        }
    }

    // Check self cycles (a box that needs to take and keep the same number)
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        for (int c : boxes[i]) {
            if (processed.count(c)) continue;
            long long required_num = T - sum_i[i] + c;
            if (required_num == c) {
                // Check if sum_i[i] - c + c == T
                if (sum_i[i] == T) {
                    valid_cycles.push_back({{make_tuple(i, c, i)}, 1 << i});
                    processed.insert(c);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // Now perform DP
    vector<int> dp(1 << k, 0);
    vector<pair<int, int>> parent(1 << k, {-1, -1});
    dp[0] = 1;

    for (int mask = 0; mask < (1 << k); ++mask) {
        if (dp[mask]) {
            for (int i = 0; i < valid_cycles.size(); ++i) {
                const auto &cycle = valid_cycles[i];
                if ((mask & cycle.mask) == 0) {
                    int new_mask = mask | cycle.mask;
                    if (!dp[new_mask]) {
                        dp[new_mask] = 1;
                        parent[new_mask] = {mask, i};
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    int full_mask = (1 << k) - 1;
    if (!dp[full_mask]) {
        cout << "No\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<CycleInfo> solution_cycles;
    int current_mask = full_mask;
    while (current_mask != 0) {
        auto [prev_mask, cycle_idx] = parent[current_mask];
        solution_cycles.push_back(valid_cycles[cycle_idx]);
        current_mask = prev_mask;
    }

    vector<pair<int, int>> result(k);
    for (const auto &ci : solution_cycles) {
        for (const auto &step : ci.steps) {
            int box, num, next_box;
            tie(box, num, next_box) = step;
            result[box] = {num, next_box + 1}; // converting to 1-based
        }
    }

    cout << "Yes\n";
    for (auto [c, p] : result) {
        cout << c << " " << p << "\n";
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

int main() {
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<vector<int>> a(n, vector<int>(n));
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < n; ++j) {
            cin >> a[i][j];
        }
    }

    map<int, vector<pair<int, int>>> s_to_pairs;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = i+1; j < n; ++j) {
            s_to_pairs[a[i][j]].emplace_back(i, j);
        }
    }

    vector<int> parent_dsu;
    vector<int> salary;
    vector<int> tree_parent;
    parent_dsu.reserve(2 * n);
    salary.reserve(2 * n);
    tree_parent.reserve(2 * n);

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        parent_dsu.push_back(i);
        salary.push_back(a[i][i]);
        tree_parent.push_back(-1);
    }

    int node_counter = n;

    for (auto& entry : s_to_pairs) {
        int s = entry.first;
        auto& pairs = entry.second;

        unordered_map<int, vector<int>> adj;
        for (auto& p : pairs) {
            int u = p.first;
            int v = p.second;

            int root_u = parent_dsu[u];
            while (parent_dsu[root_u] != root_u) {
                root_u = parent_dsu[root_u];
            }
            int root_v = parent_dsu[v];
            while (parent_dsu[root_v] != root_v) {
                root_v = parent_dsu[root_v];
            }

            if (root_u == root_v) continue;
            if (salary[root_u] >= s || salary[root_v] >= s) continue;

            adj[root_u].push_back(root_v);
            adj[root_v].push_back(root_u);
        }

        unordered_set<int> visited;
        for (auto& kv : adj) {
            int start = kv.first;
            if (visited.count(start)) continue;

            queue<int> q;
            q.push(start);
            visited.insert(start);
            vector<int> component;
            component.push_back(start);

            while (!q.empty()) {
                int u = q.front();
                q.pop();

                for (int v : adj[u]) {
                    if (!visited.count(v)) {
                        visited.insert(v);
                        component.push_back(v);
                        q.push(v);
                    }
                }
            }

            int new_node = node_counter++;
            parent_dsu.push_back(new_node);
            salary.push_back(s);
            tree_parent.push_back(-1);

            for (int node : component) {
                tree_parent[node] = new_node;
                parent_dsu[node] = new_node;
                int current = node;
                while (parent_dsu[current] != new_node) {
                    int next = parent_dsu[current];
                    parent_dsu[current] = new_node;
                    current = next;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    int k = node_counter;
    vector<int> c(k);
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        c[i] = salary[i];
    }

    int root = -1;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        if (tree_parent[i] == -1) {
            root = i;
            break;
        }
    }

    vector<pair<int, int>> edges;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        if (tree_parent[i] != -1) {
            edges.emplace_back(i + 1, tree_parent[i] + 1);
        }
    }

    cout << k << endl;
    for (int x : c) {
        cout << x << " ";
    }
    cout << endl << root + 1 << endl;
    for (auto& e : edges) {
        cout << e.first << " " << e.second << endl;
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

using ll = long long;

map<int, ll> events;

double simulate(int l, int r, ll v) {
    double current_time = l;
    ll current_volume = v;
    ll current_speed = 0;

    auto it_start = events.lower_bound(l);
    auto it_end = events.upper_bound(r);

    vector<pair<int, ll>> active_events;
    for (auto it = it_start; it != it_end; ++it) {
        active_events.emplace_back(it->first, it->second);
    }

    for (const auto& [t, s] : active_events) {
        if (t < current_time) continue;

        ll delta_time = t - current_time;
        if (delta_time > 0) {
            if (current_speed == 0) {
                if (current_volume <= 0) {
                    return current_time;
                }
            } else {
                if (current_speed < 0) {
                    double time_needed = (0.0 - current_volume) / current_speed;
                    if (time_needed <= delta_time) {
                        if (current_volume + current_speed * time_needed <= 0) {
                            return current_time + time_needed;
                        }
                    }
                }
                current_volume += current_speed * delta_time;
                if (current_volume <= 0) {
                    return current_time + (delta_time + (0.0 - current_volume + current_speed * delta_time) / (-current_speed));
                }
            }
        }

        current_time = t;
        current_speed = s;

        if (current_volume <= 0) {
            return current_time;
        }
    }

    ll delta_time = r - current_time;
    if (delta_time > 0) {
        if (current_speed == 0) {
            if (current_volume <= 0) {
                return current_time;
            }
        } else {
            if (current_speed < 0) {
                double time_needed = (0.0 - current_volume) / current_speed;
                if (time_needed <= delta_time) {
                    return current_time + time_needed;
                }
            }
            current_volume += current_speed * delta_time;
            if (current_volume <= 0) {
                return current_time + (delta_time + (0.0 - current_volume + current_speed * delta_time) / (-current_speed));
            }
        }
    }

    return -1;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int q;
    cin >> q;

    while (q--) {
        int type;
        cin >> type;

        if (type == 1) {
            int t, s;
            cin >> t >> s;
            events[t] = s;
        } else if (type == 2) {
            int t;
            cin >> t;
            events.erase(t);
        } else {
            int l, r, v;
            cin >> l >> r >> v;
            double ans = simulate(l, r, v);
            if (ans < 0) {
                cout << "-1\n";
            } else {
                cout << fixed << setprecision(10) << ans << '\n';
            }
        }
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

bool check(const vector<int>& candidate, const vector<vector<int>>& copies) {
    for (const auto& s : copies) {
        int cnt = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < candidate.size(); ++i) {
            if (candidate[i] != s[i]) {
                ++cnt;
                if (cnt > 2) break;
            }
        }
        if (cnt > 2) return false;
    }
    return true;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);
    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;
    vector<vector<int>> copies(n, vector<int>(m));
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < m; ++j) {
            cin >> copies[i][j];
        }
    }

    // Check first copy
    if (check(copies[0], copies)) {
        cout << "Yes\n";
        for (int x : copies[0]) cout << x << ' ';
        return 0;
    }

    // Check second copy
    if (check(copies[1], copies)) {
        cout << "Yes\n";
        for (int x : copies[1]) cout << x << ' ';
        return 0;
    }

    // Compute differing positions between first and second copy
    vector<int> diff_pos;
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        if (copies[0][i] != copies[1][i]) {
            diff_pos.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    int d = diff_pos.size();
    if (d > 4) {
        cout << "No\n";
        return 0;
    }

    // Generate all possible candidates by changing up to two positions
    for (int k = max(d - 2, 0); k <= min(2, d); ++k) {
        if (k == 0 || k == d) continue; // Already checked

        if (k == 1) {
            for (int pos : diff_pos) {
                vector<int> candidate = copies[0];
                candidate[pos] = copies[1][pos];
                if (check(candidate, copies)) {
                    cout << "Yes\n";
                    for (int x : candidate) cout << x << ' ';
                    return 0;
                }
            }
        } else if (k == 2) {
            for (int i = 0; i < diff_pos.size(); ++i) {
                for (int j = i + 1; j < diff_pos.size(); ++j) {
                    vector<int> candidate = copies[0];
                    candidate[diff_pos[i]] = copies[1][diff_pos[i]];
                    candidate[diff_pos[j]] = copies[1][diff_pos[j]];
                    if (check(candidate, copies)) {
                        cout << "Yes\n";
                        for (int x : candidate) cout << x << ' ';
                        return 0;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    cout << "No\n";
    return 0;
}<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a binary search tree (BST) class named `BinaryTree` that supports the following operations:
- Insert a new integer value into the tree.
- Perform an in-order traversal of the tree and return the values in a list.
- Find and return the minimum value in the tree.

Your implementation should be efficient and handle edge cases such as inserting duplicate values or finding the minimum in an empty tree.

**Input:**
- The `main` function will create a `BinaryTree` instance, insert a series of integers, and then perform the in-order traversal and find the minimum value.

**Output:**
- The in-order traversal of the tree as a list of integers.
- The minimum value in the tree.

**Sample Input:**
- Insert values: `[5, 3, 7, 2, 4, 6, 8]`

**Sample Output:**
- In-order traversal: `[2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]`
- Minimum value: `2`

```python
class TreeNode:
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value
        self.left = None
        self.right = None

class BinaryTree:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = None

    def insert(self, value):
        """Inserts a new value into the binary search tree."""
        if not self.root:
            self.root = TreeNode(value)
        else:
            self._insert_recursively(self.root, value)

    def _insert_recursively(self, node, value):
        """Helper method to insert a value recursively."""
        if value < node.value:
            if node.left is None:
                node.left = TreeNode(value)
            else:
                self._insert_recursively(node.left, value)
        else:
            if node.right is None:
                node.right = TreeNode(value)
            else:
                self._insert_recursively(node.right, value)

    def in_order_traversal(self):
        """Performs an in-order traversal of the tree and returns the values in a list."""
        elements = []
        self._in_order_traversal_recursively(self.root, elements)
        return elements

    def _in_order_traversal_recursively(self, node, elements):
        """Helper method to perform in-order traversal recursively."""
        if node:
            self._in_order_traversal_recursively(node.left, elements)
            elements.append(node.value)
            self._in_order_traversal_recursively(node.right, elements)

    def find_minimum(self):
        """Finds and returns the minimum value in the tree."""
        if not self.root:
            return None
        current = self.root
        while current.left:
            current = current.left
        return current.value

def main():
    # Create a binary tree and insert some values
    binary_tree = BinaryTree()
    values = [5, 3, 7, 2, 4, 6, 8]
    for value in values:
        binary_tree.insert(value)

    # Perform in-order traversal
    print("In-order traversal of the binary tree:", binary_tree.in_order_traversal())

    # Find the minimum value in the binary tree
    print("Minimum value in the binary tree:", binary_tree.find_minimum())

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with developing a Python script that continuously monitors the system's CPU and memory usage. The script should log these metrics to a file every 5 seconds, with each log entry timestamped. Use the `psutil` library to gather system information. Your script should be able to handle indefinite monitoring until manually stopped.

**Input:**
- No explicit input is required. The script will monitor system resources continuously.

**Output:**
- The script should output log entries to a file named `system_monitor.log` in the following format:
  ```
  <timestamp> - CPU Usage: <cpu_usage>% | Memory Usage: <memory_usage>%
  ```
  where `<timestamp>` is the current date and time, `<cpu_usage>` is the CPU usage percentage, and `<memory_usage>` is the memory usage percentage.

**Example Output:**
```
2023-10-05 14:23:15 - CPU Usage: 12% | Memory Usage: 34%
2023-10-05 14:23:20 - CPU Usage: 15% | Memory Usage: 35%
2023-10-05 14:23:25 - CPU Usage: 10% | Memory Usage: 33%
```

**Instructions:**
- Install the `psutil` library if not already installed: `pip install psutil`
- Run the script to start monitoring.
- Logs are written to `system_monitor.log` in the same directory.
- Use `Ctrl+C` to stop the script.

```python
import psutil
import time
import datetime

def monitor_system_resources(interval=5, logfile='system_monitor.log'):
    """
    Continuously monitors the CPU and memory usage of the system and logs the 
    data to a file with timestamps every 'interval' seconds.

    :param interval: Time interval (in seconds) between log entries.
    :param logfile: Name of the log file.
    """
    with open(logfile, 'a') as file:
        while True:
            timestamp = datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
            cpu_usage = psutil.cpu_percent(interval=1)
            memory_usage = psutil.virtual_memory().percent
            log_entry = f"{timestamp} - CPU Usage: {cpu_usage}% | Memory Usage: {memory_usage}%\n"
            file.write(log_entry)
            print(log_entry.strip())
            time.sleep(interval)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Setting up system resource monitoring...")
    print("* Install 'psutil' library if you haven't already: `pip install psutil`")
    print("* Run this script to start monitoring your system's CPU and memory usage.")
    print("* The logs are written to 'system_monitor.log' in the same directory as this script.")
    print("* Use Ctrl+C to stop the script when you need to.")
    print("Starting to monitor now...\n")
    monitor_system_resources()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BookstoreInventory` class to manage a bookstore's inventory. The class should support the following operations:

1. **Add a Book**: Add a new book to the inventory with its title, author, and stock count. If the book already exists, raise a `ValueError`.
2. **Remove a Book**: Remove a book from the inventory by its title. If the book does not exist, raise a `KeyError`.
3. **Search for a Book**: Search for a book by either its title or author. If searching by title and the book does not exist, return an empty dictionary. If searching by author, return all books by that author.
4. **Update Stock**: Update the stock count of a book by its title. If the book does not exist, raise a `KeyError`.
5. **List Books**: Return a dictionary of all books in the inventory.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Input: `add_book("The Great Gatsby", "F. Scott Fitzgerald", 12)`
- Input: `add_book("1984", "George Orwell", 8)`
- Input: `search_book(title="1984")`
- Output: `{'1984': ('George Orwell', 8)}`
- Input: `search_book(author="F. Scott Fitzgerald")`
- Output: `{'The Great Gatsby': ('F. Scott Fitzgerald', 12)}`
- Input: `update_stock("1984", 10)`
- Input: `list_books()`
- Output: `{'The Great Gatsby': ('F. Scott Fitzgerald', 12), '1984': ('George Orwell', 10)}`
- Input: `remove_book("The Great Gatsby")`
- Input: `list_books()`
- Output: `{'1984': ('George Orwell', 10)}`

```python
class BookstoreInventory:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize the inventory dictionary."""
        self.inventory = {}

    def add_book(self, title, author, stock_count):
        """Add a new book to the inventory. Raise ValueError if the book already exists."""
        if title in self.inventory:
            raise ValueError("Book already exists in inventory.")
        self.inventory[title] = (author, stock_count)

    def remove_book(self, title):
        """Remove a book from the inventory. Raise KeyError if the book does not exist."""
        if title not in self.inventory:
            raise KeyError("Book not found in inventory.")
        del self.inventory[title]

    def search_book(self, title=None, author=None):
        """Search for a book by title or author. Return an empty dictionary if searching by title and the book does not exist.
        Return all books by the author if searching by author. Raise ValueError if neither title nor author is provided."""
        if title:
            return {title: self.inventory[title]} if title in self.inventory else {}
        elif author:
            return {k: v for k, v in self.inventory.items() if v[0] == author}
        else:
            raise ValueError("Either title or author must be provided for search.")

    def update_stock(self, title, stock_count):
        """Update the stock count of a book. Raise KeyError if the book does not exist."""
        if title not in self.inventory:
            raise KeyError("Book not found in inventory.")
        author, _ = self.inventory[title]
        self.inventory[title] = (author, stock_count)

    def list_books(self):
        """Return a dictionary of all books in the inventory."""
        return self.inventory
```<#sep#>You are given a dictionary `employees` where each key is an employee ID and each value is another dictionary containing the employee's name, department, and salary. Your task is to implement a function `average_salary_by_department` that computes the average salary for each department and returns a dictionary with department names as keys and their corresponding average salaries as values.

**Input:**
- A dictionary `employees` with the following structure:
  - Keys: Employee IDs (integers).
  - Values: Dictionaries with keys `"name"`, `"department"`, and `"salary"`.

**Output:**
- A dictionary where keys are department names (strings) and values are the average salaries (floats) of employees in each department.

**Sample Input:**
```python
employees = {
    1: {"name": "Alice", "department": "Engineering", "salary": 90000},
    2: {"name": "Bob", "department": "HR", "salary": 75000},
    3: {"name": "Charlie", "department": "Engineering", "salary": 85000},
    4: {"name": "David", "department": "HR", "salary": 70000},
    5: {"name": "Eva", "department": "Marketing", "salary": 65000}
}
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
{'Engineering': 87500.0, 'HR': 72500.0, 'Marketing': 65000.0}
```

```python
def average_salary_by_department(employees):
    """
    Computes the average salary for each department from the given employees dictionary.

    Parameters:
    employees (dict): A dictionary where keys are employee IDs and values are dictionaries
                      containing employee information including 'name', 'department', and 'salary'.

    Returns:
    dict: A dictionary with department names as keys and their average salaries as values.
    """
    department_salaries = {}
    
    for emp_id, emp_info in employees.items():
        department = emp_info['department']
        salary = emp_info['salary']
        
        if department not in department_salaries:
            department_salaries[department] = {'total_salary': 0, 'count': 0}
        
        department_salaries[department]['total_salary'] += salary
        department_salaries[department]['count'] += 1
    
    average_salaries = {
        department: info['total_salary'] / info['count']
        for department, info in department_salaries.items()
    }
    
    return average_salaries

# Example usage
employees = {
    1: {"name": "Alice", "department": "Engineering", "salary": 90000},
    2: {"name": "Bob", "department": "HR", "salary": 75000},
    3: {"name": "Charlie", "department": "Engineering", "salary": 85000},
    4: {"name": "David", "department": "HR", "salary": 70000},
    5: {"name": "Eva", "department": "Marketing", "salary": 65000}
}

print(average_salary_by_department(employees))
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple linear regression model using gradient descent in Python. Your implementation should include a class `LinearRegression` with the following methods:
- `fit(X, y)`: Fits the model to the given data.
- `predict(X)`: Predicts the target values for the given input data.
- `mean_squared_error(y_true, y_pred)`: Computes the mean squared error between the true and predicted values.
- `r_squared(y_true, y_pred)`: Computes the R-squared value between the true and predicted values.

Your class should be able to handle datasets with multiple features. The main section of your script should demonstrate the usage of the `LinearRegression` class with the following sample input and output:

**Sample Input:**
```python
X = np.array([[1], [2], [3], [4], [5]])
y = np.array([2, 4, 6, 8, 10])
X_test = np.array([[6], [7]])
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Predicted values: [12. 14.]
Mean Squared Error: 0.0
R-squared: 1.0
```

```python
import numpy as np

class LinearRegression:
    def __init__(self, learning_rate=0.01, iterations=1000):
        """
        Initializes the Linear Regression model with a given learning rate and number of iterations.
        
        :param learning_rate: Step size for the gradient descent algorithm.
        :param iterations: Number of iterations for the gradient descent algorithm.
        """
        self.learning_rate = learning_rate
        self.iterations = iterations
        self.weights = None
        self.bias = None

    def fit(self, X, y):
        """
        Fits the linear regression model to the provided data using gradient descent.
        
        :param X: Input features (numpy array).
        :param y: Target values (numpy array).
        """
        n_samples, n_features = X.shape
        self.weights = np.zeros(n_features)
        self.bias = 0
        
        for _ in range(self.iterations):
            y_predicted = np.dot(X, self.weights) + self.bias
            
            dw = (1 / n_samples) * np.dot(X.T, (y_predicted - y))
            db = (1 / n_samples) * np.sum(y_predicted - y)
            
            self.weights -= self.learning_rate * dw
            self.bias -= self.learning_rate * db

    def predict(self, X):
        """
        Predicts the target values for the provided input data.
        
        :param X: Input features (numpy array).
        :return: Predicted target values (numpy array).
        """
        return np.dot(X, self.weights) + self.bias

    def mean_squared_error(self, y_true, y_pred):
        """
        Computes the mean squared error between the true and predicted values.
        
        :param y_true: True target values (numpy array).
        :param y_pred: Predicted target values (numpy array).
        :return: Mean squared error (float).
        """
        return np.mean((y_true - y_pred) ** 2)

    def r_squared(self, y_true, y_pred):
        """
        Computes the R-squared value between the true and predicted values.
        
        :param y_true: True target values (numpy array).
        :param y_pred: Predicted target values (numpy array).
        :return: R-squared value (float).
        """
        ss_t = np.sum((y_true - np.mean(y_true)) ** 2)
        ss_r = np.sum((y_true - y_pred) ** 2)
        return 1 - (ss_r / ss_t)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    # Sample dataset
    X = np.array([[1], [2], [3], [4], [5]])
    y = np.array([2, 4, 6, 8, 10])

    # Create and train model
    model = LinearRegression(learning_rate=0.01, iterations=1000)
    model.fit(X, y)

    # Predicting values
    X_test = np.array([[6], [7]])
    predicted_y = model.predict(X_test)
    print(f"Predicted values: {predicted_y}")

    # Performance Metrics
    y_pred = model.predict(X)
    mse = model.mean_squared_error(y, y_pred)
    r2 = model.r_squared(y, y_pred)
    print(f"Mean Squared Error: {mse}")
    print(f"R-squared: {r2}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Rectangle` class in Python that models a rectangle in a 2D plane. The class should have the following attributes and methods:

- **Attributes:**
  - `width`: A positive number representing the width of the rectangle.
  - `height`: A positive number representing the height of the rectangle.

- **Methods:**
  - `area()`: Returns the area of the rectangle.
  - `perimeter()`: Returns the perimeter of the rectangle.
  - `scale(factor)`: Scales the rectangle by a given positive factor, multiplying both the width and height by this factor.
  - `rotate()`: Rotates the rectangle 90 degrees clockwise by swapping its width and height.

The constructor should validate the input values for `width` and `height`, raising a `ValueError` with the message "Width and height must be positive numbers." if either is non-positive. Similarly, the `scale` method should raise a `ValueError` with the message "Scale factor must be a positive number." if the factor is non-positive.

**Sample Input/Output:**
- Input: `rect = Rectangle(3, 4)`
- Output: `rect.area() -> 12`, `rect.perimeter() -> 14`, `rect.scale(2)`, `rect.area() -> 48`, `rect.rotate()`, `rect.width -> 14`, `rect.height -> 24`

```python
class Rectangle:
    def __init__(self, width, height):
        """
        Initializes a Rectangle with given width and height.
        
        :param width: A positive number representing the width of the rectangle.
        :param height: A positive number representing the height of the rectangle.
        :raises ValueError: If width or height is not a positive number.
        """
        if width <= 0 or height <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Width and height must be positive numbers.")
        self.width = width
        self.height = height

    def area(self):
        """
        Calculates the area of the rectangle.
        
        :return: The area of the rectangle.
        """
        return self.width * self.height

    def perimeter(self):
        """
        Calculates the perimeter of the rectangle.
        
        :return: The perimeter of the rectangle.
        """
        return 2 * (self.width + self.height)

    def scale(self, factor):
        """
        Scales the rectangle by a given factor.
        
        :param factor: A positive number representing the scaling factor.
        :raises ValueError: If the factor is not a positive number.
        """
        if factor <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Scale factor must be a positive number.")
        self.width *= factor
        self.height *= factor

    def rotate(self):
        """
        Rotates the rectangle 90 degrees clockwise by swapping its width and height.
        """
        self.width, self.height = self.height, self.width
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Polynomial` class in Python that represents a polynomial in one variable. The class should support the following operations:

1. **Addition** (`+`): Add two polynomials.
2. **Subtraction** (`-`): Subtract one polynomial from another.
3. **Multiplication** (`*`): Multiply two polynomials.
4. **Division** (`/`): Divide one polynomial by another, returning both the quotient and remainder.
5. **Evaluation**: Evaluate the polynomial at a given point.
6. **Root Finding**: Find the roots of the polynomial.
7. **String Representation**: Print the polynomial in a standard form.

Your class should be able to handle polynomials of arbitrary degree. Implement a test script to demonstrate the functionality of your class.

**Sample Input/Output:**

For the polynomials \( P(x) = 1 + 2x + 3x^2 \) and \( Q(x) = 1 + 3x \):

- **Addition**: \( P(x) + Q(x) = 2 + 5x + 3x^2 \)
- **Subtraction**: \( P(x) - Q(x) = 3x^2 - x \)
- **Multiplication**: \( P(x) \times Q(x) = 1 + 5x + 12x^2 + 9x^3 \)
- **Division**: \( P(x) \div Q(x) \) yields quotient \( 3x - 1 \) and remainder \( 4 \)
- **Evaluation at \( x = 2 \)**: \( P(2) = 17 \), \( Q(2) = 7 \)
- **Roots**: Roots of \( P(x) \) are approximately \(-0.3473\) and \(-0.9527\), roots of \( Q(x) \) are approximately \(-0.3333\)

```python
import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P

class Polynomial:
    """
    Represents a polynomial in one variable.
    """
    
    def __init__(self, coefficients):
        """
        Initialize a polynomial with a list of coefficients.
        Coefficient list is in the form [a0, a1, a2, ..., an] where
        a0 + a1*x + a2*x^2 + ... + an*x^n.
        """
        self.coefficients = np.array(coefficients)

    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Add two polynomials.
        """
        return Polynomial(np.polyadd(self.coefficients, other.coefficients))

    def __sub__(self, other):
        """
        Subtract two polynomials.
        """
        return Polynomial(np.polysub(self.coefficients, other.coefficients))

    def __mul__(self, other):
        """
        Multiply two polynomials.
        """
        return Polynomial(np.polymul(self.coefficients, other.coefficients))
    
    def __truediv__(self, other):
        """
        Divide two polynomials, return quotient and remainder.
        """
        quot, rem = np.polydiv(self.coefficients, other.coefficients)
        return Polynomial(list(map(np.rint, quot))), Polynomial(list(map(np.rint, rem)))

    def evaluate(self, x):
        """
        Evaluate the polynomial at a given point x.
        """
        return np.polyval(self.coefficients, x)

    def find_roots(self):
        """
        Find the roots of the polynomial.
        """
        return P(self.coefficients).roots()

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Print the polynomial in a standard form.
        """
        terms = []
        for power, coeff in enumerate(self.coefficients):
            if power == 0:
                terms.append(f"{coeff:.0f}")
            elif power == 1:
                terms.append(f"{coeff:.0f}x")
            else:
                terms.append(f"{coeff:.0f}x^{power}")
        
        terms = [term for term in reversed(terms) if not term.startswith("0")]
        
        if not terms:
            return "0"
        elif len(terms) == 1:
            return terms[0]
        else:
            return " + ".join(terms).replace("+ -", "- ")

# Test script
if __name__ == "__main__":
    poly1 = Polynomial([1, 2, 3])  # Represents 1 + 2x + 3x^2
    poly2 = Polynomial([1, 3])     # Represents 1 + 3x
    
    # Print polynomials
    print(f"Polynomial 1: {poly1}")
    print(f"Polynomial 2: {poly2}")

    # Evaluate polynomials at x = 2
    print(f"Evaluation of Polynomial 1 at x = 2: {poly1.evaluate(2)}")
    print(f"Evaluation of Polynomial 2 at x = 2: {poly2.evaluate(2)}")

    # Find roots of polynomials
    print(f"Roots of Polynomial 1: {poly1.find_roots()}")
    print(f"Roots of Polynomial 2: {poly2.find_roots()}")

    # Perform arithmetic operations
    add_result = poly1 + poly2
    sub_result = poly1 - poly2
    mul_result = poly1 * poly2
    div_quot, div_rem = poly1 / poly2

    print(f"Adding: {add_result}")
    print(f"Subtracting: {sub_result}")
    print(f"Multiplying: {mul_result}")
    print(f"Dividing: Quotient {div_quot}, Remainder {div_rem}")
```<#sep#>You are given a dataset representing customer purchase history. The dataset is structured as a list of lists, where each sublist contains three elements: `[customer_id, product_id, purchase_date]`. Your task is to generate a report for each customer. The report should include the total number of unique products purchased by the customer and the earliest and latest purchase dates. 

**Input:**
- A list of lists `dataset` where each sublist is of the form `[customer_id, product_id, purchase_date]`. 
- `purchase_date` is a string in the format `YYYY-MM-DD`.

**Output:**
- A dictionary where the keys are `customer_id` and the values are dictionaries containing:
  - `total_unique_products`: An integer representing the number of unique products purchased by the customer.
  - `purchase_dates`: A tuple of two strings representing the earliest and latest purchase dates in the format `YYYY-MM-DD`.

**Sample Input:**
```python
dataset = [
    [1, 101, '2021-01-01'],
    [1, 102, '2021-01-05'],
    [2, 101, '2021-01-02'],
    [1, 101, '2021-01-03'],
    [2, 103, '2021-01-01'],
    [1, 104, '2021-01-07'],
]
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
{
    1: {'total_unique_products': 3, 'purchase_dates': ('2021-01-01', '2021-01-07')},
    2: {'total_unique_products': 2, 'purchase_dates': ('2021-01-01', '2021-01-02')}
}
```

```python
from datetime import datetime

def generate_customer_purchase_report(dataset):
    """
    Generates a purchase report for each customer based on the provided dataset.
    
    Parameters:
    dataset (list of lists): Each sublist contains [customer_id, product_id, purchase_date].
    
    Returns:
    dict: A dictionary with customer_id as keys and a dictionary of report details as values.
    """
    report = {}

    for customer_id, product_id, purchase_date in dataset:
        purchase_date = datetime.strptime(purchase_date, '%Y-%m-%d')

        if customer_id not in report:
            report[customer_id] = {
                'total_unique_products': set(),
                'purchase_dates': (purchase_date, purchase_date)
            }

        report[customer_id]['total_unique_products'].add(product_id)

        current_earliest, current_latest = report[customer_id]['purchase_dates']
        report[customer_id]['purchase_dates'] = (
            min(current_earliest, purchase_date),
            max(current_latest, purchase_date)
        )

    for customer_id, data in report.items():
        data['total_unique_products'] = len(data['total_unique_products'])
        data['purchase_dates'] = tuple(date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d') for date in data['purchase_dates'])

    return report
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class in Python. The class should support the following operations:
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance. The amount must be positive.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance. The amount must be positive and should not exceed the current balance.
- **Check Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.
- **Transfer**: Transfer a specified amount from the current account to another `BankAccount` instance. The amount must be positive and should not exceed the current balance.

The class should have the following attributes:
- `account_holder_name`: A string representing the name of the account holder.
- `balance`: A float representing the current balance of the account, initialized to 0.0 if not provided.

For each operation, if the input is invalid (e.g., negative amount, insufficient funds), the method should raise a `ValueError` with an appropriate message.

**Sample Input/Output**:
```python
account1 = BankAccount("Alice", 1000)
account2 = BankAccount("Bob", 500)
account1.deposit(500)  # Balance of account1 should be 1500
account1.withdraw(200)  # Balance of account1 should be 1300
account1.check_balance()  # Should return 1300
account1.transfer(account2, 300)  # Balance of account1 should be 1000, balance of account2 should be 800
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_holder_name, initial_balance=0.0):
        """
        Initialize a new BankAccount instance.

        :param account_holder_name: The name of the account holder.
        :param initial_balance: The initial balance of the account, default is 0.0.
        """
        self.account_holder_name = account_holder_name
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account.

        :param amount: The amount to deposit, must be positive.
        :return: The updated balance of the account.
        :raises ValueError: If the amount is not positive.
        """
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Deposit amount must be positive")
        self.balance += amount
        return self.balance

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account.

        :param amount: The amount to withdraw, must be positive and not exceed the current balance.
        :return: The updated balance of the account.
        :raises ValueError: If the amount is not positive or exceeds the current balance.
        """
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Withdrawal amount must be positive")
        if amount > self.balance:
            raise ValueError("Insufficient funds for this withdrawal")
        self.balance -= amount
        return self.balance

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Check the current balance of the account.

        :return: The current balance of the account.
        """
        return self.balance

    def transfer(self, target_account, amount):
        """
        Transfer a specified amount from the current account to another BankAccount instance.

        :param target_account: The target BankAccount instance to transfer funds to.
        :param amount: The amount to transfer, must be positive and not exceed the current balance.
        :return: A tuple containing the updated balances of the current and target accounts.
        :raises ValueError: If the target account is not a BankAccount instance, the amount is not positive, or exceeds the current balance.
        """
        if not isinstance(target_account, BankAccount):
            raise ValueError("Target account must be an instance of BankAccount")
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Transfer amount must be positive")
        if amount > self.balance:
            raise ValueError("Insufficient funds for this transfer")
        
        self.withdraw(amount)
        target_account.deposit(amount)
        return self.balance, target_account.balance

Your task is to implement a function `filter_products_by_category` that filters the products based on a specified category. The function should return a list of dictionaries representing the filtered products. If the input list is empty or no products match the specified category, the function should return an empty list.

**Input:**
- A list of dictionaries `products` where each dictionary contains the keys `id`, `name`, `price`, and `category`.
- A string `category` representing the category to filter products by.

**Output:**
- A list of dictionaries representing the filtered products.

**Sample Input:**
```python

    {"id": 1, "name": "Laptop", "price": 1200, "category": "Electronics"},
    {"id": 2, "name": "Smartphone", "price": 800, "category": "Electronics"},
    {"id": 3, "name": "Shirt", "price": 20, "category": "Clothing"},
    {"id": 4, "name": "Trousers", "price": 50, "category": "Clothing"}

    {"id": 1, "name": "Laptop", "price": 1200, "category": "Electronics"},
    {"id": 2, "name": "Smartphone", "price": 800, "category": "Electronics"}
]
```

```python
def filter_products_by_category(products, category):
    """
    Filters a list of products by category.

    Args:
    - products: List of dictionaries, each dictionary representing a product with 'id', 'name', 'price', and 'category' keys.
    - category: String representing the category to filter products by.

    Returns:
    - A list of dictionaries representing the filtered products. Returns an empty list if no products match the category or if the products list is empty.
    """
    if not products:
        return []

    return [product for product in products if product['category'] == category]

# Example usage:
products = [
    {"id": 1, "name": "Laptop", "price": 1200, "category": "Electronics"},
    {"id": 2, "name": "Smartphone", "price": 800, "category": "Electronics"},
    {"id": 3, "name": "Shirt", "price": 20, "category": "Clothing"},
    {"id": 4, "name": "Trousers", "price": 50, "category": "Clothing"}
]

# should return laptop and smartphone
print(filter_products_by_category(products, 'Electronics'))

# should return Shirt and Trousers
print(filter_products_by_category(products, 'Clothing'))

# should return empty list for non-existent category
print(filter_products_by_category(products, 'Furniture'))

# should return empty list for empty products list
print(filter_products_by_category([], 'Electronics'))
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a program to generate a random maze using the recursive backtracking algorithm. The maze should be represented as a 2D grid of size `(2 * width + 1) x (2 * height + 1)`, where `0` represents an open path and `1` represents a wall. The entrance is located at the top-left corner (second cell of the first row) and the exit is located at the bottom-right corner (second-to-last cell of the last row). Your program should include a function to display the maze.

**Input:**
- Two integers `width` and `height` (1 ≤ width, height ≤ 20) representing the dimensions of the maze.

**Output:**
- A 2D grid representing the maze, where `0` is an open path and `1` is a wall.

**Sample Input:**
```
3 3
```

**Sample Output:**
```
# # # # # # # 
#     #     # 
# ### ### # 
# #     #   # 
# ### ### ### 
#     #     # 
# # # # # # # 
```

```python
import random

class MazeGenerator:
    def __init__(self, width, height):
        """
        Initializes the maze generator with given width and height.
        
        :param width: The width of the maze (number of cells).
        :param height: The height of the maze (number of cells).
        """
        self.width = width
        self.height = height
        self.maze = [[1] * (2 * self.width + 1) for _ in range(2 * self.height + 1)]

    def generate(self):
        """
        Generates the maze using the recursive backtracking algorithm.
        """
        self._create_entrance_exit()
        self._recursive_backtracking((1, 1))

    def _create_entrance_exit(self):
        """
        Creates the entrance and exit of the maze.
        """
        self.maze[0][1] = 0
        self.maze[-1][-2] = 0

    def _recursive_backtracking(self, position):
        """
        Recursively carves out the maze using the backtracking algorithm.
        
        :param position: The current position in the maze.
        """
        directions = [(0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)]
        random.shuffle(directions)

        for dx, dy in directions:
            nx, ny = position[0] + 2 * dx, position[1] + 2 * dy
            if 0 < nx < 2 * self.width + 1 and 0 < ny < 2 * self.height + 1 and self.maze[ny][nx] == 1:
                self.maze[ny][nx] = 0
                self.maze[position[1] + dy][position[0] + dx] = 0
                self._recursive_backtracking((nx, ny))

    def display(self):
        """
        Displays the maze.
        """
        for row in self.maze:
            print(''.join(['#' if cell == 1 else ' ' for cell in row]))

# Example usage:
width = 3  # width of the maze (<width> cells)
height = 3 # height of the maze (<height> cells)
maze = MazeGenerator(width, height)
maze.generate()
maze.display()
```<#sep#>You are given a tree data structure where each node can have zero or more children. Each node has an integer value and a list of children. Implement a class `TreeNode` to represent a node in the tree. The `TreeNode` class should have the following attributes and methods:
- `value`: the integer value stored in the node.
- `children`: a list of child nodes.
- `add_child(child)`: adds a child node to the list of children.
- `remove_child(child)`: removes a child node from the list of children.
- `get_value()`: returns the integer value stored in the node.
- `get_children()`: returns the list of child nodes.

Additionally, implement a function `inorder_traversal(root)` that performs an in-order traversal of the tree and returns a list of node values in the order they were visited. In the context of a general tree (not a binary tree), visit all children of a node in the order they appear in the children list.

**Sample Input:**
```
root = TreeNode(1)
child1 = TreeNode(2)
child2 = TreeNode(3)
child3 = TreeNode(4)
child4 = TreeNode(5)
root.add_child(child1)
root.add_child(child2)
child1.add_child(child3)
child1.add_child(child4)
```

**Sample Output:**
```
[4, 5, 2, 3, 1]
```

```python
class TreeNode:
    def __init__(self, value):
        """
        Initializes a new TreeNode with the given value and an empty list of children.
        
        :param value: The integer value to be stored in the node.
        """
        self.value = value
        self.children = []

    def add_child(self, child):
        """
        Adds a child node to the list of children.
        
        :param child: The TreeNode to be added as a child.
        """
        if isinstance(child, TreeNode):
            self.children.append(child)

    def remove_child(self, child):
        """
        Removes a child node from the list of children if it exists.
        
        :param child: The TreeNode to be removed from the children list.
        """
        if child in self.children:
            self.children.remove(child)

    def get_value(self):
        """
        Returns the integer value stored in the node.
        
        :return: The integer value of the node.
        """
        return self.value

    def get_children(self):
        """
        Returns the list of child nodes.
        
        :return: A list of TreeNode objects representing the children.
        """
        return self.children

def inorder_traversal(root):
    """
    Performs an in-order traversal of the tree and returns a list of node values in the order they were visited.
    
    :param root: The root TreeNode of the tree.
    :return: A list of integers representing the node values in in-order traversal.
    """
    result = []
    def traverse(node):
        if node:
            for child in node.get_children():
                traverse(child)
            result.append(node.get_value())
    
    traverse(root)
    return result

The program should interact with the user through a text-based interface. The user will input commands to perform these operations. The program should handle invalid inputs gracefully.

**Input:**
- The first line contains an integer `n` (1 ≤ n ≤ 100), the number of operations to perform.
- The next `n` lines each contain an operation in the format:
  - `deposit X` where `X` is a positive number representing the amount to deposit.
  - `withdraw X` where `X` is a positive number representing the amount to withdraw.
  - `check_balance` to display the current balance.

**Output:**
- For each `deposit` and `withdraw` operation, print the updated balance after the operation.
- For each `check_balance` operation, print the current balance.
- If an invalid operation is attempted (e.g., negative deposit/withdraw amount, insufficient funds), print an appropriate error message.

**Sample Input:**
```
5
deposit 100
withdraw 50
check_balance
withdraw 60
check_balance
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Deposited $100.00, new balance is $100.00.

```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, initial_balance=0):
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account.
        :param amount: The amount to deposit (must be positive).
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            print(f"Deposited ${amount:.2f}, new balance is ${self.balance:.2f}.")
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account.
        :param amount: The amount to withdraw (must be positive and less than or equal to the current balance).
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if self.balance >= amount:
                self.balance -= amount
                print(f"Withdrew ${amount:.2f}, new balance is ${self.balance:.2f}.")
            else:
                print("Insufficient funds.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Check and print the current balance of the account.
        """
        print(f"Current balance is ${self.balance:.2f}.")

def main():
    account = BankAccount(0)  # Start with a balance of 0
    n = int(input().strip())
    for _ in range(n):
        command = input().strip().split()
        if command[0] == 'deposit':
            try:
                amount = float(command[1])
                account.deposit(amount)
            except ValueError:
                print("Invalid input. Please enter a number.")
        elif command[0] == 'withdraw':
            try:
                amount = float(command[1])
                account.withdraw(amount)
            except ValueError:
                print("Invalid input. Please enter a number.")
        elif command[0] == 'check_balance':
            account.check_balance()
        else:
            print("Invalid command.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with developing a program to generate random sentences using a Markov chain. Given a text file `corpus.txt` containing a large corpus of text, implement a function `generate_sentences(filename, sentence_count, sentence_length)` that reads the text file, constructs a Markov chain, and generates `sentence_count` random sentences, each of length `sentence_length`. Sentences are considered to be terminated by a period, exclamation mark, or question mark. Assume that the text file `corpus.txt` is in the same directory as your Python script.

**Input:**
- `filename`: A string representing the name of the text file.
- `sentence_count`: An integer representing the number of sentences to generate.
- `sentence_length`: An integer representing the length of each sentence.

**Output:**
- A string containing `sentence_count` sentences, each of length `sentence_length`, separated by newlines.

**Sample Input:**
`corpus.txt` contains:
```
Hello world! This is a test. Hello again!
```
`sentence_count = 2`
`sentence_length = 5`

**Sample Output:**
```
Hello world! This
is a test. Hello
```

```python
import random
import re
from collections import defaultdict

def generate_sentences(filename, sentence_count, sentence_length):
    """
    Generates random sentences using a Markov chain from a given text file.

    :param filename: The name of the text file containing the corpus.
    :param sentence_count: The number of sentences to generate.
    :param sentence_length: The length of each sentence.
    :return: A string containing the generated sentences.
    """
    with open(filename, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as file:
        text = file.read().lower()

    words = re.findall(r'\b\w+\b|[.!?]', text)
    
    markov_chain = defaultdict(list)
    for i in range(len(words) - 1):
        current_word = words[i]
        next_word = words[i + 1]
        markov_chain[current_word].append(next_word)

    def generate_sentence(markov_chain, sentence_length):
        starting_words = [word for word in markov_chain.keys() if word not in '.!?']
        current_word = random.choice(starting_words)
        sentence = [current_word.capitalize()]
        
        while len(sentence) < sentence_length:
            next_possible_words = markov_chain.get(current_word, [])
            if not next_possible_words:
                break
            current_word = random.choice(next_possible_words)
            sentence.append(current_word)
            if current_word in '.!?':
                sentence.append(random.choice(starting_words).capitalize())

        if sentence[-1] not in '.!?':
            sentence.append(random.choice('.!?'))

        return ' '.join(sentence)

    sentences = [generate_sentence(markov_chain, sentence_length) for _ in range(sentence_count)]
    return '\n'.join(sentences)

# Example usage
filename = "corpus.txt"
sentence_count = 3
sentence_length = 10
print(generate_sentences(filename, sentence_count, sentence_length))
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing an `Inventory` class for a store management system. The class should support the following operations:

1. **Add Item**: Add a new item to the inventory or update the quantity of an existing item. Each item is identified by its name, has a price, and a quantity.
2. **Remove Item**: Remove a specified quantity of an item from the inventory. If the specified quantity exceeds the available quantity, print an error message. If the item does not exist, print an error message.
3. **Get Inventory**: Return the current state of the inventory as a dictionary.
4. **Get Total Value**: Calculate and return the total value of the inventory, which is the sum of the product of the price and quantity for each item.

**Input/Output Specification**:
- The `Inventory` class will be tested with a series of operations. The operations will be provided as method calls on an instance of the `Inventory` class.
- For each `remove_item` operation that fails due to insufficient quantity or non-existent item, an error message should be printed.
- The `get_inventory` method should return a dictionary where keys are item names and values are dictionaries containing the price and quantity of the item.
- The `get_total_value` method should return a float representing the total value of the inventory.

**Sample Input/Output**:
```python
inventory = Inventory()
inventory.add_item("apple", 0.5, 10)
inventory.add_item("banana", 0.3, 20)
print(inventory.get_inventory())  # {'apple': {'price': 0.5, 'quantity': 10}, 'banana': {'price': 0.3, 'quantity': 20}}
print(inventory.get_total_value())  # 11.0
inventory.remove_item("apple", 5)
print(inventory.get_inventory())  # {'apple': {'price': 0.5, 'quantity': 5}, 'banana': {'price': 0.3, 'quantity': 20}}
print(inventory.get_total_value())  # 8.5
inventory.remove_item("apple", 10)  # Error: Not enough apple in inventory to remove.
inventory.remove_item("orange", 5)  # Error: orange not found in inventory.
```

```python
class Inventory:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty dictionary to store items."""
        self.items = {}

    def add_item(self, name, price, quantity):
        """Add or update an item in the inventory."""
        if name in self.items:
            self.items[name]['quantity'] += quantity
        else:
            self.items[name] = {'price': price, 'quantity': quantity}

    def remove_item(self, name, quantity):
        """Remove a certain quantity of an item from the inventory."""
        if name in self.items:
            if self.items[name]['quantity'] > quantity:
                self.items[name]['quantity'] -= quantity
            elif self.items[name]['quantity'] == quantity:
                del self.items[name]
            else:
                print(f"Error: Not enough {name} in inventory to remove.")
        else:
            print(f"Error: {name} not found in inventory.")

    def get_inventory(self):
        """Return the current inventory."""
        return self.items

    def get_total_value(self):
        """Calculate the total value of the inventory."""
        total_value = 0
        for item in self.items.values():
            total_value += item['price'] * item['quantity']
        return total_value
