```python
class Employee:
    """
    A class to represent an employee.

    Attributes:
    name (str): The name of the employee.
    age (int): The age of the employee.
    department (str): The department where the employee works.
    salary (float): The salary of the employee.
    """

    def __init__(self, name, age, department, salary):
        """
        Constructs all the necessary attributes for the employee object.

        Args:
        name (str): The name of the employee.
        age (int): The age of the employee.
        department (str): The department where the employee works.
        salary (float): The salary of the employee.
        """
        self.name = name
        self.age = age
        self.department = department
        self.salary = salary

    def get_details(self):
        """
        Returns a formatted string containing the employee's details.

        Returns:
        str: A string representing the employee's details.
        """
        return f"Name: {self.name}, Age: {self.age}, Department: {self.department}, Salary: ${self.salary:,.2f}"

    def give_raise(self, percentage):
        """
        Increases the employee's salary by a given percentage.

        Args:
        percentage (float): The percentage by which to increase the salary.
        """
        self.salary *= (1 + percentage / 100)

    def promote(self, new_department):
        """
        Changes the employee's department to a new one.

        Args:
        new_department (str): The new department for the employee.
        """
        self.department = new_department
```<#sep#>You are given a JSON file named `data.json` containing a list of dictionaries, each representing a person with a `job_title` field. Your task is to write a Python script that reads this file, extracts all unique job titles, and writes them to a file named `job_titles.txt` in alphabetical order. Each job title should be on a new line.

**Sample Input (`data.json`):**
```json
[
    {"name": "Alice", "job_title": "Engineer"},
    {"name": "Bob", "job_title": "Designer"},
    {"name": "Charlie", "job_title": "Engineer"},
    {"name": "David", "job_title": "Manager"}
]
```

**Sample Output (`job_titles.txt`):**
```
Designer
Engineer
Manager
```

```python
import json
import os

def read_json_file(file_path):
    """
    Reads a JSON file and returns the data as a Python object.
    
    Args:
        file_path (str): The path to the JSON file.
    
    Returns:
        dict or list: The data from the JSON file, or None if an error occurred.
    """
    try:
        with open(file_path, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as file:
            return json.load(file)
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Error: The file {file_path} does not exist.")
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        print(f"Error: The file {file_path} is not a valid JSON file.")
    return None

def extract_unique_job_titles(data):
    """
    Extracts unique job titles from a list of dictionaries.
    
    Args:
        data (list): A list of dictionaries, each containing a 'job_title' key.
    
    Returns:
        set: A set of unique job titles.
    """
    job_titles = set()
    if isinstance(data, list):
        for item in data:
            if isinstance(item, dict) and 'job_title' in item:
                job_titles.add(item['job_title'])
    return job_titles

def write_job_titles_to_file(job_titles, output_file_path):
    """
    Writes a set of job titles to a file, one per line, in alphabetical order.
    
    Args:
        job_titles (set): A set of job titles.
        output_file_path (str): The path to the output file.
    """
    try:
        with open(output_file_path, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as file:
            for title in sorted(job_titles):
                file.write(title + '\n')
    except IOError:
        print(f"Error: Could not write to file {output_file_path}.")

def main():
    input_file_path = 'data.json'
    output_file_path = 'job_titles.txt'

    if not os.path.exists(input_file_path):
        print(f"The file {input_file_path} does not exist.")
        return

    data = read_json_file(input_file_path)
    if data is None:
        return

    unique_job_titles = extract_unique_job_titles(data)
    write_job_titles_to_file(unique_job_titles, output_file_path)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```<#sep#>You are given a string representing a mathematical expression. The expression consists of non-negative integers, operators (`+`, `-`, `*`, `/`), parentheses `(` and `)`, and spaces. Your task is to evaluate the expression and return the result as a floating-point number. The expression should be evaluated according to the standard operator precedence and parentheses rules. If the expression is invalid, your function should raise a `ValueError`.

**Input:**
- A string `expression` (1 ≤ len(expression) ≤ 100) representing the mathematical expression.

**Output:**
- A floating-point number representing the result of the evaluated expression.

**Sample Input:**
```
"3 + 5 * (2 - 8) / 4"
```

**Sample Output:**
```
-7.0
```

```python
def evaluate_expression(expression):
    """
    Evaluates a mathematical expression provided as a string.
    
    Args:
    expression (str): A string representing the mathematical expression.
    
    Returns:
    float: The result of the evaluated expression.
    
    Raises:
    ValueError: If the expression is invalid.
    """
    def apply_operator(operators, values):
        operator = operators.pop()
        right = values.pop()
        left = values.pop()
        if operator == '+':
            values.append(left + right)
        elif operator == '-':
            values.append(left - right)
        elif operator == '*':
            values.append(left * right)
        elif operator == '/':
            if right == 0:
                raise ValueError("Division by zero")
            values.append(left / right)
        else:
            raise ValueError("Invalid operator")

    def greater_precedence(op1, op2):
        precedence = {'+': 1, '-': 1, '*': 2, '/': 2}
        return precedence[op1] > precedence[op2]

    expression = expression.replace(' ', '')
    operators = []
    values = []
    i = 0

    while i < len(expression):
        if expression[i] == '(':
            operators.append(expression[i])
        elif expression[i] == ')':
            while operators and operators[-1] != '(':
                apply_operator(operators, values)
            if operators and operators[-1] == '(':
                operators.pop()
            else:
                raise ValueError("Mismatched parentheses")
        elif expression[i] in '+-*/':
            while (operators and operators[-1] != '(' and
                   greater_precedence(operators[-1], expression[i])):
                apply_operator(operators, values)
            operators.append(expression[i])
        elif expression[i].isdigit() or expression[i] == '.':
            j = i
            while j < len(expression) and (expression[j].isdigit() or expression[j] == '.'):
                j += 1
            values.append(float(expression[i:j]))
            i = j - 1
        else:
            raise ValueError("Invalid character in expression")
        i += 1

    while operators:
        op = operators.pop()
        if op == '(' or op == ')':
            raise ValueError("Mismatched parentheses")
        apply_operator(operators, values)

    if len(values) != 1:
        raise ValueError("Invalid expression")

    return values[0]

# Test
expression = "3 + 5 * (2 - 8) / 4"
result = evaluate_expression(expression)
print(result)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class in Python that supports basic banking operations. The class should allow deposits, withdrawals, and balance checks. Additionally, it should maintain a transaction history and ensure that the account balance never goes negative. 

**Operations:**
- `deposit(amount)`: Adds the specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
- `withdraw(amount)`: Subtracts the specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
- `check_balance()`: Returns the current balance of the account.
- `print_transaction_history()`: Prints all transactions (deposits and withdrawals) along with the final balance.

**Constraints:**
- The account balance must not be negative after any transaction.
- All monetary amounts should be handled as positive numbers.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
account = BankAccount(100)
account.deposit(50)
account.withdraw(30)
account.print_transaction_history()
```
**Expected Output:**
```
Transaction History:
Deposited: $50.00
Withdrew: $30.00
Current Balance: $120.00
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, initial_balance=0):
        self.balance = initial_balance
        self.transaction_history = []

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Adds the specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
        
        :param amount: The amount to deposit.
        :raises ValueError: If the deposit amount is not positive.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            self.transaction_history.append(f'Deposited: ${amount:.2f}')
        else:
            raise ValueError("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Subtracts the specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
        
        :param amount: The amount to withdraw.
        :raises ValueError: If the withdrawal amount is not positive or if there are insufficient funds.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if amount <= self.balance:
                self.balance -= amount
                self.transaction_history.append(f'Withdrew: ${amount:.2f}')
            else:
                raise ValueError("Insufficient funds for withdrawal.")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Returns the current balance of the account.
        
        :return: A string representing the current balance.
        """
        return f'Current Balance: ${self.balance:.2f}'

    def print_transaction_history(self):
        """
        Prints all transactions (deposits and withdrawals) along with the final balance.
        """
        print("Transaction History:")
        for transaction in self.transaction_history:
            print(transaction)
        print(self.check_balance())
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a Python class `DatabaseConnection` that manages SQLite database connections. The class should support opening and closing connections, executing SQL queries, and handling exceptions. Additionally, it should implement the context manager protocol to allow usage with a `with` statement. The class should provide the following methods:

- `__init__(self, db_name)`: Initializes the database connection with the provided database name.
- `open(self)`: Opens the database connection.
- `close(self)`: Closes the database connection.
- `execute(self, query, params=None)`: Executes a given SQL query with optional parameters and returns the cursor object. If an error occurs, it should print an error message and return `None`.
- `__enter__(self)`: Returns the `DatabaseConnection` object when used with a `with` statement.
- `__exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb)`: Closes the database connection when the block of code is exited and returns `False` to propagate any exceptions.

**Sample Input/Output:**
- Input: 
  ```python
  with DatabaseConnection('example.db') as db:
      query = "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS test (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, data TEXT)"
      db.execute(query)
      query = "INSERT INTO test (data) VALUES (?)"
      db.execute(query, ("example data",))
      query = "SELECT * FROM test"
      results = db.execute(query)
      for row in results:
          print(row)
  ```
- Output:
  ```
  Opened database connection to example.db
  Closed database connection to example.db
  (1, 'example data')
  ```

```python
import sqlite3

class DatabaseConnection:
    def __init__(self, db_name):
        """
        Initializes the database connection with the provided database name.
        
        :param db_name: Name of the SQLite database file.
        """
        self.db_name = db_name
        self.connection = None

    def open(self):
        """
        Opens the database connection.
        """
        try:
            self.connection = sqlite3.connect(self.db_name)
            print(f"Opened database connection to {self.db_name}")
        except sqlite3.Error as e:
            print(f"Failed to open database connection: {e}")

    def close(self):
        """
        Closes the database connection.
        """
        if self.connection:
            self.connection.close()
            print(f"Closed database connection to {self.db_name}")
            self.connection = None

    def execute(self, query, params=None):
        """
        Executes a given SQL query with optional parameters.
        
        :param query: SQL query to be executed.
        :param params: Optional parameters for the SQL query.
        :return: Cursor object if successful, None otherwise.
        """
        try:
            cursor = self.connection.cursor()
            if params:
                cursor.execute(query, params)
            else:
                cursor.execute(query)
            self.connection.commit()
            return cursor
        except sqlite3.Error as e:
            print(f"Failed to execute query: {e}")
            return None

    def __enter__(self):
        """
        Returns the DatabaseConnection object when used with a `with` statement.
        
        :return: DatabaseConnection object.
        """
        self.open()
        return self

    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
        """
        Closes the database connection when the block of code is exited.
        
        :param exc_type: Exception type.
        :param exc_val: Exception value.
        :param exc_tb: Exception traceback.
        :return: False to propagate any exceptions.
        """
        self.close()
        return False  # Propagate exceptions if they occur
```<#sep#>You are tasked with designing a `Book` class to manage a library's inventory. The class should support the following operations:

- **Initialization**: Create a book with a title, author, publication year, ISBN, and the number of available copies.

- **Return Book**: Increase the number of available copies by one.

Implement the `Book` class with the methods described above. Your implementation should pass the following test cases:

**Sample Input/Output:**
```
Input:

book.borrow_book()

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, publication_year, isbn, available_copies):
        """
        Initialize a new book with the given details.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param publication_year: The year the book was published.
        :param isbn: The ISBN number of the book.
        :param available_copies: The number of copies available in the library.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.publication_year = publication_year
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.available_copies = available_copies

    def borrow_book(self):
        """
        Borrow a book if available. Otherwise, print a message indicating the book is not available.
        """
        if self.available_copies > 0:
            self.available_copies -= 1
        else:
            print("The book is not available.")

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Return a book, increasing the number of available copies by one.
        """
        self.available_copies += 1

    def is_available(self):
        """
        Check if the book is available.

        :return: True if the book has at least one available copy, otherwise False.
        """
        return self.available_copies > 0

# Example usage:
book_example = Book("1984", "George Orwell", 1949, "978-0451524935", 3)
print(book_example.is_available())  # True
book_example.borrow_book()
print(book_example.available_copies)  # 2
book_example.return_book()
print(book_example.available_copies)  # 3
book_example.borrow_book()
book_example.borrow_book()
book_example.borrow_book()
book_example.borrow_book()  # Output: The book is not available.
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Polynomial` class in Python that supports basic arithmetic operations and evaluation. The class should be able to handle polynomials represented by a list of coefficients in descending order of powers. Implement the following methods:

- `__init__(self, coefficients)`: Initializes the polynomial with a list of coefficients.
- `__add__(self, other)`: Adds two polynomials.
- `__sub__(self, other)`: Subtracts one polynomial from another.
- `__mul__(self, other)`: Multiplies two polynomials.
- `evaluate(self, x)`: Evaluates the polynomial at a given value of `x`.

**Sample Input/Output:**

For the polynomials `3x^2 + 2` and `x - 1`:

- Addition: `(3x^2 + 2) + (x - 1) = 3x^2 + x + 1`
- Subtraction: `(3x^2 + 2) - (x - 1) = 3x^2 - x + 3`
- Multiplication: `(3x^2 + 2) * (x - 1) = 3x^3 - 3x^2 + 2x - 2`
- Evaluation at `x=2`: `3x^2 + 2` evaluated at `x=2` is `14`

```python
class Polynomial:
    def __init__(self, coefficients):
        """
        Initializes the polynomial with a list of coefficients.
        Coefficients are in descending order of powers.
        """
        self.coefficients = coefficients
    
    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Adds two polynomials.
        """
        max_len = max(len(self.coefficients), len(other.coefficients))
        coeffs_self = [0] * (max_len - len(self.coefficients)) + self.coefficients
        coeffs_other = [0] * (max_len - len(other.coefficients)) + other.coefficients
        result = [a + b for a, b in zip(coeffs_self, coeffs_other)]
        return Polynomial(result)
    
    def __sub__(self, other):
        """
        Subtracts one polynomial from another.
        """
        max_len = max(len(self.coefficients), len(other.coefficients))
        coeffs_self = [0] * (max_len - len(self.coefficients)) + self.coefficients
        coeffs_other = [0] * (max_len - len(other.coefficients)) + other.coefficients
        result = [a - b for a, b in zip(coeffs_self, coeffs_other)]
        return Polynomial(result)
    
    def __mul__(self, other):
        """
        Multiplies two polynomials.
        """
        result = [0] * (len(self.coefficients) + len(other.coefficients) - 1)
        for i, coeff1 in enumerate(self.coefficients):
            for j, coeff2 in enumerate(other.coefficients):
                result[i + j] += coeff1 * coeff2
        return Polynomial(result)
    
    def evaluate(self, x):
        """
        Evaluates the polynomial at a given value of x.
        """
        total = 0
        for power, coeff in enumerate(reversed(self.coefficients)):
            total += coeff * (x ** power)
        return total

    def __str__(self):
        terms = []
        for power, coeff in enumerate(reversed(self.coefficients)):
            if coeff == 0: 
                continue
            sign = '+' if coeff >= 0 else '-'
            coeff = abs(coeff)
            if power == 0:
                term = f'{int(coeff)}'
            elif power == 1:
                term = f'{" " if coeff == 1 else f"{int(coeff)}"}x'
            else:
                term = f'{" " if coeff == 1 else f"{int(coeff)}"}x^{power}'
            terms.append(f' {sign} {term}' if terms else (f'{int(coeff)}x^{power}' if power != 0 else f'{int(coeff)}'))
        return ''.join(terms)


# Example usage
p1 = Polynomial([3, 0, 2])  # 3x^2 + 2
p2 = Polynomial([1, -1])    # x - 1

p3 = p1 + p2  # (3x^2 + 2) + (x - 1) = 3x^2 + x + 1
p4 = p1 - p2  # (3x^2 + 2) - (x - 1) = 3x^2 - x + 3
p5 = p1 * p2  # (3x^2 + 2) * (x - 1) = 3x^3 - 3x^2 + 2x - 2

print("p1:", p1)  # Output: 3x^2 + 2
print("p2:", p2)  # Output: x - 1
print("p3:", p3)  # Output: 3x^2 + x + 1
print("p4:", p4)  # Output: 3x^2 - x + 3
print("p5:", p5)  # Output: 3x^3 - 3x^2 + 2x - 2

print("p1 evaluated at x=2:", p1.evaluate(2))  # Output: 14

Your program should repeatedly prompt the user to input two numbers and an operation, then output the result of the operation. The operations are denoted as follows:

- `2` for subtraction
- `3` for multiplication

The program should handle invalid inputs gracefully by prompting the user again. Division by zero should be handled by returning the message "Error! Division by zero."

```

3
2
7
3
4
2

yes

```python
def add(x, y):
    """Return the sum of x and y."""
    return x + y

def subtract(x, y):
    """Return the difference of x and y."""
    return x - y

def multiply(x, y):
    """Return the product of x and y."""
    return x * y

def divide(x, y):
    """Return the quotient of x and y, or an error message if y is zero."""
    if y == 0:
        return "Error! Division by zero."
    else:
        return x / y

def calculator():
    """Run a simple calculator that performs basic arithmetic operations."""
    print("Simple Calculator")
    print("Select operation:")
    print("1. Add")
    print("2. Subtract")
    print("3. Multiply")
    print("4. Divide")
    
    while True:
        choice = input("Enter choice(1/2/3/4): ")

        if choice in ('1', '2', '3', '4'):
            try:
                num1 = float(input("Enter first number: "))
                num2 = float(input("Enter second number: "))
            except ValueError:
                print("Invalid input! Please enter a number.")
                continue
            
            if choice == '1':
                print(f"{num1} + {num2} = {add(num1, num2)}")
            
            elif choice == '2':
                print(f"{num1} - {num2} = {subtract(num1, num2)}")
            
            elif choice == '3':
                print(f"{num1} * {num2} = {multiply(num1, num2)}")
            
            elif choice == '4':
                print(f"{num1} / {num2} = {divide(num1, num2)}")
        else:
            print("Invalid input! Please enter a valid choice.")

        next_calculation = input("Do you want to perform another calculation? (yes/no): ")
        if next_calculation.lower() != 'yes':
            break

if __name__ == "__main__":
    calculator()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Matrix` class in Python that supports basic matrix operations. The class should handle the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: Create a matrix from a 2D list of numbers.
- **String Representation**: Provide a string representation of the matrix.
- **Transpose**: Return a new matrix that is the transpose of the current matrix.
- **Addition**: Add two matrices of the same dimensions and return a new matrix.
- **Multiplication**: Multiply two matrices where the number of columns in the first matrix equals the number of rows in the second matrix, and return a new matrix.
- **Determinant**: Calculate and return the determinant of a square matrix.
- **Inverse**: Calculate and return the inverse of a square matrix if it exists; otherwise, raise a `ValueError`.
- **Equality Check**: Check if two matrices are equal.

**Input/Output Specification**:
- For the `__str__` method, the output should be a string representation of the matrix.
- For the `transpose`, `add`, `multiply`, `determinant`, and `inverse` methods, the output should be a new `Matrix` object or a scalar value as specified.
- For the `__eq__` method, the output should be a boolean value indicating whether the matrices are equal.

**Sample Input/Output**:
```python
m1 = Matrix([[1, 2], [3, 4]])
m2 = Matrix([[5, 6], [7, 8]])

print(m1)  # Output: [[1. 2.]
           #         [3. 4.]]

print(m1.transpose())  # Output: [[1. 3.]
                       #         [2. 4.]]

print(m1.add(m2))  # Output: [[ 6.  8.]
                   #         [10. 12.]]

print(m1.multiply(m2))  # Output: [[19. 22.]
                        #         [43. 50.]]

print(m1.determinant())  # Output: -2.0

print(m1.inverse())  # Output: [[-2.   1. ]
                     #         [ 1.5 -0.5]]

print(m1 == m2)  # Output: False
```

```python
import numpy as np

class Matrix:
    def __init__(self, data):
        """
        Initializes the matrix with a 2D list of numbers.
        """
        self.data = np.array(data, dtype=float)
        self.rows, self.cols = self.data.shape

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Returns a string representation of the matrix.
        """
        return str(self.data)

    def transpose(self):
        """
        Returns a new matrix that is the transpose of the current matrix.
        """
        transposed_data = self.data.T
        return Matrix(transposed_data)

    def add(self, other):
        """
        Adds two matrices and returns a new matrix.
        Raises ValueError if matrices are not of the same size.
        """
        if self.rows != other.rows or self.cols != other.cols:
            raise ValueError("Matrices must be of the same size to add.")
        result_data = self.data + other.data
        return Matrix(result_data)

    def multiply(self, other):
        """
        Multiplies two matrices and returns a new matrix.
        Raises ValueError if the number of columns in the first matrix
        is not equal to the number of rows in the second matrix.
        """
        if self.cols != other.rows:
            raise ValueError("Number of columns in the first matrix must be equal to the number of rows in the second matrix.")
        result_data = np.dot(self.data, other.data)
        return Matrix(result_data)

    def determinant(self):
        """
        Returns the determinant of the matrix.
        Raises ValueError if the matrix is not square.
        """
        if self.rows != self.cols:
            raise ValueError("Determinant can only be calculated for square matrices.")
        return np.linalg.det(self.data)

    def inverse(self):
        """
        Returns the inverse of the matrix if it exists.
        Raises ValueError if the matrix is not square or is singular.
        """
        if self.rows != self.cols:
            raise ValueError("Inverse can only be calculated for square matrices.")
        det = self.determinant()
        if det == 0:
            raise ValueError("Matrix is singular and cannot be inverted.")
        inv_data = np.linalg.inv(self.data)
        return Matrix(inv_data)

    def __eq__(self, other):
        """
        Checks if two matrices are equal.
        """
        if not isinstance(other, Matrix):
            return False
        return np.array_equal(self.data, other.data)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with designing a RESTful API using Flask to manage a collection of books. The API must support the following operations:

1. **GET /books**: Retrieve a list of all books.
2. **GET /books/{book_id}**: Retrieve a specific book by its unique identifier.
3. **POST /books**: Add a new book to the collection.
4. **PUT /books/{book_id}**: Update an existing book's details by its unique identifier.
5. **DELETE /books/{book_id}**: Remove a book from the collection by its unique identifier.

Each book is represented by the following attributes:
- `id` (int): A unique identifier for the book.
- `title` (string): The title of the book.
- `author` (string): The author of the book.
- `published_year` (int): The year the book was published.

**Sample Input/Output:**

- **GET /books**
  - Response: `[
      {"id": 1, "title": "1984", "author": "George Orwell", "published_year": 1949},
      {"id": 2, "title": "The Great Gatsby", "author": "F. Scott Fitzgerald", "published_year": 1925},
      {"id": 3, "title": "To Kill a Mockingbird", "author": "Harper Lee", "published_year": 1960}
    ]`

- **GET /books/1**
  - Response: `{"id": 1, "title": "1984", "author": "George Orwell", "published_year": 1949}`

- **POST /books**
  - Request Body: `{"title": "Brave New World", "author": "Aldous Huxley", "published_year": 1932}`
  - Response: `{"id": 4, "title": "Brave New World", "author": "Aldous Huxley", "published_year": 1932}`

- **PUT /books/1**
  - Request Body: `{"title": "Nineteen Eighty-Four"}`
  - Response: `{"id": 1, "title": "Nineteen Eighty-Four", "author": "George Orwell", "published_year": 1949}`

- **DELETE /books/1**
  - Response: `{"result": true}`

```python
from flask import Flask, jsonify, request, abort

app = Flask(__name__)

# Sample data representing a list of books
books = [
    {'id': 1, 'title': '1984', 'author': 'George Orwell', 'published_year': 1949},
    {'id': 2, 'title': 'The Great Gatsby', 'author': 'F. Scott Fitzgerald', 'published_year': 1925},
    {'id': 3, 'title': 'To Kill a Mockingbird', 'author': 'Harper Lee', 'published_year': 1960}
]

def find_book(book_id):
    """Helper function to find a book by its ID."""
    return next((book for book in books if book['id'] == book_id), None)

@app.route('/books', methods=['GET'])
def get_books():
    """Retrieve a list of all books."""
    return jsonify(books)

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['GET'])
def get_book(book_id):
    """Retrieve a specific book by its ID."""
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404)
    return jsonify(book)

@app.route('/books', methods=['POST'])
def add_book():
    """Add a new book to the list."""
    if not request.json or 'title' not in request.json or 'author' not in request.json or 'published_year' not in request.json:
        abort(400)
    new_book = {
        'id': books[-1]['id'] + 1 if books else 1,
        'title': request.json['title'],
        'author': request.json['author'],
        'published_year': request.json['published_year']
    }
    books.append(new_book)
    return jsonify(new_book), 201

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['PUT'])
def update_book(book_id):
    """Update an existing book by its ID."""
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404)
    if not request.json:
        abort(400)
    book['title'] = request.json.get('title', book['title'])
    book['author'] = request.json.get('author', book['author'])
    book['published_year'] = request.json.get('published_year', book['published_year'])
    return jsonify(book)

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['DELETE'])
def delete_book(book_id):
    """Delete a book by its ID."""
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404)
    books.remove(book)
    return jsonify({'result': True})

@app.errorhandler(404)
def not_found(error):
    """Error handler for 404 Not Found."""
    return jsonify({'error': 'Not found'}), 404

@app.errorhandler(400)
def bad_request(error):
    """Error handler for 400 Bad Request."""
    return jsonify({'error': 'Bad request'}), 400

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app.run(debug=True)
```<#sep#>You are given the coordinates of `n` cities on a 2D plane. Your task is to implement a genetic algorithm to find the shortest possible route that visits each city exactly once and returns to the starting city. The genetic algorithm should include the following components:
- A population of candidate solutions (routes).
- A fitness function to evaluate the quality of each solution.
- Selection, crossover, and mutation operators to generate new solutions.
- A termination condition to stop the algorithm after a fixed number of generations.

**Input:**
- An integer `n` representing the number of cities.
- A list of `n` tuples, each containing the (x, y) coordinates of a city.

**Output:**
- The best route found as a list of city indices.
- The total distance of the best route.

**Sample Input:**
```
5
0 0
1 5
5 2
6 6
8 3
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Best route found: [0, 1, 3, 2, 4]
Best distance: 21.732050807568876
```

```python
import random
import math

# Constants
POPULATION_SIZE = 100
GENERATIONS = 500
MUTATION_RATE = 0.01
TOURNAMENT_SIZE = 3

def distance(city1, city2):
    """Calculate the Euclidean distance between two cities."""
    return math.sqrt((city1[0] - city2[0]) ** 2 + (city1[1] - city2[1]) ** 2)

def calculate_total_distance(route, cities):
    """Calculate the total distance of a given route."""
    total_distance = 0
    num_cities = len(route)
    for i in range(num_cities):
        total_distance += distance(cities[route[i]], cities[route[(i + 1) % num_cities]])
    return total_distance

def initialize_population(num_individuals, num_cities):
    """Initialize a random population of routes."""
    population = []
    for _ in range(num_individuals):
        individual = list(range(num_cities))
        random.shuffle(individual)
        population.append(individual)
    return population

def fitness(route, cities):
    """Evaluate the fitness of a route based on its total distance."""
    return 1 / calculate_total_distance(route, cities)

def select(population, cities):
    """Select individuals for the next generation using tournament selection."""
    selected = []
    for _ in range(len(population)):
        tournament = random.sample(population, TOURNAMENT_SIZE)
        tournament_sorted = sorted(tournament, key=lambda x: fitness(x, cities), reverse=True)
        selected.append(tournament_sorted[0])
    return selected

def crossover(parent1, parent2):
    """Perform crossover between two parent routes to produce a child route."""
    size = len(parent1)
    start, end = sorted(random.sample(range(size), 2))
    child = [None] * size
    child[start:end+1] = parent1[start:end+1]
    pointer = end + 1 if end + 1 < size else 0
    for city in parent2:
        if city not in child:
            if pointer >= size:
                pointer = 0
            child[pointer] = city
            pointer += 1
    return child

def mutate(route, mutation_rate):
    """Mutate a route by swapping two cities with a given probability."""
    for i in range(len(route)):
        if random.random() < mutation_rate:
            j = random.randint(0, len(route) - 1)
            route[i], route[j] = route[j], route[i]

def genetic_algorithm(cities):
    """Run the genetic algorithm to find the shortest route."""
    population = initialize_population(POPULATION_SIZE, len(cities))
    best_route = min(population, key=lambda x: calculate_total_distance(x, cities))
    best_distance = calculate_total_distance(best_route, cities)

    for generation in range(GENERATIONS):
        population = select(population, cities)
        next_generation = []

        while len(next_generation) < POPULATION_SIZE:
            parent1, parent2 = random.sample(population, 2)
            child = crossover(parent1, parent2)
            mutate(child, MUTATION_RATE)
            next_generation.append(child)

        population = next_generation
        current_best = min(population, key=lambda x: calculate_total_distance(x, cities))
        current_best_distance = calculate_total_distance(current_best, cities)
        
        if current_best_distance < best_distance:
            best_route = current_best
            best_distance = current_best_distance
            print(f"Generation {generation + 1}: Best distance = {best_distance}")

    return best_route, best_distance

# Test the genetic algorithm with the provided city coordinates
cities = [(0, 0), (1, 5), (5, 2), (6, 6), (8, 3)]
best_route, best_distance = genetic_algorithm(cities)
print(f"Best route found: {best_route}")
print(f"Best distance: {best_distance}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Matrix` class in Python that supports basic matrix operations. The class should have the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: The constructor `__init__(self, data)` should initialize the matrix with a 2D list `data`.
- **Transpose**: The method `transpose(self)` should return a new `Matrix` object that is the transpose of the original matrix.
- **Addition**: The method `__add__(self, other)` should implement matrix addition with another `Matrix` object. It should raise a `ValueError` if the matrices are not of the same dimensions.
- **Multiplication**: The method `__mul__(self, other)` should implement matrix multiplication with another `Matrix` object. It should raise a `ValueError` if the matrices are not compatible for multiplication.

**Input Format**:
- The input will be handled through the constructor and method calls.
- For example, `Matrix([[1, 2], [3, 4]])` initializes a 2x2 matrix.

**Output Format**:
- The output will be handled through the `__str__` method, which should return a string representation of the matrix.
- For example, `print(mat1 + mat2)` should print the matrix resulting from the addition of `mat1` and `mat2`.

**Sample Input**:
```python
mat1 = Matrix([[1, 2], [3, 4]])
mat2 = Matrix([[2, 0], [1, 2]])
```

**Sample Output**:
```
3 2
4 6

1 4
2 8

2 1
0 2
```

```python
class Matrix:
    def __init__(self, data):
        """
        Initialize the matrix with a 2D list `data`.
        """
        self.data = data
        self.rows = len(data)
        self.cols = len(data[0]) if self.rows > 0 else 0

    def transpose(self):
        """
        Return a new `Matrix` object that is the transpose of the original matrix.
        """
        transposed_data = [[self.data[j][i] for j in range(self.rows)] for i in range(self.cols)]
        return Matrix(transposed_data)

    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Implement matrix addition with another `Matrix` object.
        Raises ValueError if the matrices are not of the same dimensions.
        """
        if self.rows != other.rows or self.cols != other.cols:
            raise ValueError("Matrices must be of the same dimensions for addition.")
        
        added_data = [[self.data[i][j] + other.data[i][j] for j in range(self.cols)] for i in range(self.rows)]
        return Matrix(added_data)

    def __mul__(self, other):
        """
        Implement matrix multiplication with another `Matrix` object.
        Raises ValueError if the matrices are not compatible for multiplication.
        """
        if self.cols != other.rows:
            raise ValueError("Number of columns in the first matrix must be equal to number of rows in the second matrix for multiplication.")
        
        multiplied_data = [[sum(self.data[i][k] * other.data[k][j] for k in range(self.cols)) for j in range(other.cols)] for i in range(self.rows)]
        return Matrix(multiplied_data)

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Return a string representation of the matrix.
        """
        return '\n'.join([' '.join(map(str, row)) for row in self.data])
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Movie` class in Python. The class should encapsulate the properties of a movie, including its title, director, release year, and rating. Implement methods to retrieve each of these properties. Additionally, implement a static method `average_rating` that computes the average rating of a list of `Movie` objects. Your program should demonstrate the usage of this class by creating a list of `Movie` objects and printing their average rating.

**Sample Input:**
- A list of `Movie` objects with the following details:
  - "Inception", "Christopher Nolan", 2010, 8.8
  - "The Matrix", "Lana Wachowski, Lilly Wachowski", 1999, 8.7
  - "Interstellar", "Christopher Nolan", 2014, 8.6
  - "The Godfather", "Francis Ford Coppola", 1972, 9.2

**Sample Output:**
```
The average rating of the movies is: 8.85
```

```python
class Movie:
    def __init__(self, title, director, release_year, rating):
        """
        Initialize a new Movie instance.

        :param title: The title of the movie.
        :param director: The director of the movie.
        :param release_year: The release year of the movie.
        :param rating: The rating of the movie.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.director = director
        self.release_year = release_year
        self.rating = rating

    def get_title(self):
        """
        Get the title of the movie.

        :return: The title of the movie.
        """
        return self.title

    def get_director(self):
        """
        Get the director of the movie.

        :return: The director of the movie.
        """
        return self.director

    def get_release_year(self):
        """
        Get the release year of the movie.

        :return: The release year of the movie.
        """
        return self.release_year

    def get_rating(self):
        """
        Get the rating of the movie.

        :return: The rating of the movie.
        """
        return self.rating

    @staticmethod
    def average_rating(movies):
        """
        Calculate the average rating of a list of Movie objects.

        :param movies: A list of Movie objects.
        :return: The average rating of the movies.
        """
        if not movies:
            return 0
        total_rating = sum(movie.get_rating() for movie in movies)
        return total_rating / len(movies)

# Demonstration of the Movie class
movies = [
    Movie("Inception", "Christopher Nolan", 2010, 8.8),
    Movie("The Matrix", "Lana Wachowski, Lilly Wachowski", 1999, 8.7),
    Movie("Interstellar", "Christopher Nolan", 2014, 8.6),
    Movie("The Godfather", "Francis Ford Coppola", 1972, 9.2)
]

avg_rating = Movie.average_rating(movies)
print(f"The average rating of the movies is: {avg_rating:.2f}")
```<#sep#>You are given a string `expression` representing a mathematical expression consisting of integers, the four basic arithmetic operators (`+`, `-`, `*`, `/`), and parentheses. Your task is to evaluate the expression correctly handling operator precedence and parentheses.

**Input:**
- A single string `expression` (1 ≤ len(expression) ≤ 100) containing valid mathematical expression.

**Output:**
- An integer representing the result of the evaluated expression.

**Sample Input:**
```
"3 + 5 * (2 - 8)"
```

**Sample Output:**
```
-17
```

```python
def evaluate_expression(expression):
    """
    Evaluates a mathematical expression given as a string.
    
    :param expression: A string representing a mathematical expression.
    :return: An integer representing the result of the evaluated expression.
    """
    def tokenize(expr):
        """
        Tokenizes the input expression into numbers and operators.
        
        :param expr: A string representing a mathematical expression.
        :return: A list of tokens (numbers and operators).
        """
        tokens = []
        num_buffer = ''
        operators = set('+-*/()')
        for char in expr:
            if char in operators:
                if num_buffer:
                    tokens.append(float(num_buffer))
                    num_buffer = ''
                tokens.append(char)
            elif char.isdigit() or char == '.':
                num_buffer += char
        if num_buffer:
            tokens.append(float(num_buffer))
        return tokens

    def precedence(op):
        """
        Returns the precedence level of the given operator.
        
        :param op: A character representing an operator.
        :return: An integer representing the precedence level.
        """
        if op in ('+', '-'):
            return 1
        if op in ('*', '/'):
            return 2
        return 0

    def apply_operator(operators, values):
        """
        Applies the top operator from the operators stack to the top two values from the values stack.
        
        :param operators: A list representing the operators stack.
        :param values: A list representing the values stack.
        """
        operator = operators.pop()
        right = values.pop()
        left = values.pop()
        if operator == '+':
            values.append(left + right)
        elif operator == '-':
            values.append(left - right)
        elif operator == '*':
            values.append(left * right)
        elif operator == '/':
            values.append(left / right)

    def greater_precedence(op1, op2):
        """
        Compares the precedence of two operators.
        
        :param op1: A character representing the first operator.
        :param op2: A character representing the second operator.
        :return: True if op1 has greater precedence than op2, False otherwise.
        """
        return precedence(op1) > precedence(op2)

    def evaluate_tokens(tokens):
        """
        Evaluates the tokens list according to operator precedence and parentheses.
        
        :param tokens: A list of tokens (numbers and operators).
        :return: An integer representing the result of the evaluated expression.
        """
        values = []
        operators = []
        i = 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            if isinstance(tokens[i], float):
                values.append(tokens[i])
            elif tokens[i] == '(':
                operators.append(tokens[i])
            elif tokens[i] == ')':
                while operators and operators[-1] != '(':
                    apply_operator(operators, values)
                operators.pop()
            else:
                while (operators and operators[-1] != '(' and
                       greater_precedence(operators[-1], tokens[i])):
                    apply_operator(operators, values)
                operators.append(tokens[i])
            i += 1
        while operators:
            apply_operator(operators, values)
        return values[0]

    final_tokens = tokenize(expression)
    return evaluate_tokens(final_tokens)

# Usage:
expression = "3 + 5 * (2 - 8)"
result = evaluate_expression(expression)
print(int(result))  # Output should be -17
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class that supports basic banking operations. The class should have the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: The constructor should accept an account number, account holder's name, and an optional initial balance (default is 0).
- **Deposit**: A method to add a specified amount to the account balance. The method should raise a `ValueError` if the deposit amount is not positive.
- **Withdraw**: A method to subtract a specified amount from the account balance. The method should raise a `ValueError` if the withdrawal amount is not positive or if there are insufficient funds.
- **Get Balance**: A method to return the current balance of the account.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Initialize a `BankAccount` with account number "123456", account holder "John Doe", and initial balance 100.
- Deposit 50: Balance should be 150.
- Withdraw 30: Balance should be 120.
- Attempt to withdraw 150: Should raise `ValueError` with message "Insufficient funds".

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_number, account_holder, initial_balance=0):
        """
        Initialize a new bank account.

        :param account_number: A string representing the account number.
        :param account_holder: A string representing the account holder's name.
        :param initial_balance: A float representing the initial balance (default is 0).
        """
        self.account_number = account_number
        self.account_holder = account_holder
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account.

        :param amount: A float representing the amount to deposit.
        :raises ValueError: If the deposit amount is not positive.
        """
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Deposit amount must be positive")
        self.balance += amount

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account.

        :param amount: A float representing the amount to withdraw.
        :raises ValueError: If the withdrawal amount is not positive or if there are insufficient funds.
        """
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Withdrawal amount must be positive")
        if amount > self.balance:
            raise ValueError("Insufficient funds")
        self.balance -= amount

    def get_balance(self):
        """
        Get the current balance of the account.

        :return: A float representing the current balance.
        """
        return self.balance
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `product_data.csv` containing product information with the following columns: `product_id`, `product_name`, `price`, `stock_quantity`, and `category`. Your task is to write a Python script that reads this file and categorizes the products into three price ranges: `Budget` (0 - 100), `Mid-range` (101 - 500), and `Premium` (501 - 10000). The script should output the categorized products in a structured format.

**Sample Input:**
```
product_id,product_name,price,stock_quantity,category
1,Widget,50,100,Toys
2,Gadget,250,50,Electronics
3,Tool,750,20,Hardware
4,Toy,99,200,Toys
5,Device,1500,10,Electronics
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Budget Products:
Product ID: 1, Product Name: Widget, Price: 50, Stock Quantity: 100, Category: Toys
Product ID: 4, Product Name: Toy, Price: 99, Stock Quantity: 200, Category: Toys

Mid-range Products:
Product ID: 2, Product Name: Gadget, Price: 250, Stock Quantity: 50, Category: Electronics

Premium Products:
Product ID: 3, Product Name: Tool, Price: 750, Stock Quantity: 20, Category: Hardware
Product ID: 5, Product Name: Device, Price: 1500, Stock Quantity: 10, Category: Electronics
```

```python
import csv

def categorize_products(csv_file):
    """
    Reads a CSV file containing product data and categorizes products into three price ranges:
    Budget (0 - 100), Mid-range (101 - 500), and Premium (501 - 10000).
    
    Args:
    csv_file (str): The path to the CSV file containing product data.
    
    Returns:
    None: Prints the categorized products in a structured format.
    """
    budget_products = []
    mid_range_products = []
    premium_products = []

    with open(csv_file, mode='r', newline='') as file:
        reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        for row in reader:
            price = float(row['price'])
            if 0 <= price <= 100:
                budget_products.append(row)
            elif 101 <= price <= 500:
                mid_range_products.append(row)
            elif 501 <= price <= 10000:
                premium_products.append(row)

    print("Budget Products:")
    for product in budget_products:
        print(f"Product ID: {product['product_id']}, Product Name: {product['product_name']}, "
              f"Price: {product['price']}, Stock Quantity: {product['stock_quantity']}, "
              f"Category: {product['category']}")

    print("\nMid-range Products:")
    for product in mid_range_products:
        print(f"Product ID: {product['product_id']}, Product Name: {product['product_name']}, "
              f"Price: {product['price']}, Stock Quantity: {product['stock_quantity']}, "
              f"Category: {product['category']}")

    print("\nPremium Products:")
    for product in premium_products:
        print(f"Product ID: {product['product_id']}, Product Name: {product['product_name']}, "
              f"Price: {product['price']}, Stock Quantity: {product['stock_quantity']}, "
              f"Category: {product['category']}")

# Example usage:
# categorize_products('product_data.csv')
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class in Python that supports specific operations through its special methods. The class should be initialized with a title and an author. Implement the following methods:

- `__init__(self, title, author)`: Initializes the book with a title and an author.
- `__str__(self)`: Returns a string representation of the book in the format `"Title: <title>, Author: <author>"`.
- `__repr__(self)`: Returns a string that can be used to recreate the object in the format `"Book(title='<title>', author='<author>')"`.
- `__eq__(self, other)`: Compares two books for equality based on their titles and authors.

**Sample Input:**
```python
book1 = Book("1984", "George Orwell")
book2 = Book("1984", "George Orwell")
book3 = Book("Animal Farm", "George Orwell")
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell
Book(title='1984', author='George Orwell')
True
False
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance with the given title and author.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Returns a string representation of the book.

        :return: A string in the format "Title: <title>, Author: <author>".
        """
        return f"Title: {self.title}, Author: {self.author}"

    def __repr__(self):
        """
        Returns a string that can be used to recreate the book object.

        :return: A string in the format "Book(title='<title>', author='<author>')"..
        """
        return f"Book(title='{self.title}', author='{self.author}')"

    def __eq__(self, other):
        """
        Compares two books for equality based on their titles and authors.

        :param other: Another Book object to compare with.
        :return: True if both books have the same title and author, False otherwise.
        """
        if isinstance(other, Book):
            return self.title == other.title and self.author == other.author
        return False

# Example usage:
book1 = Book("1984", "George Orwell")
book2 = Book("1984", "George Orwell")
book3 = Book("Animal Farm", "George Orwell")

print(str(book1))  # Should output: Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell
print(repr(book1))  # Should output: Book(title='1984', author='George Orwell')
print(book1 == book2)  # Should output: True
print(book1 == book3)  # Should output: False
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple library management system. The system should support the following operations:

1. **Add a Book**: Add a book to the library with its title and author.
2. **Borrow a Book**: Borrow a book from the library if it is available.
3. **Return a Book**: Return a book to the library if it was borrowed.
4. **Display Books**: Display all books in the library along with their availability status.

Your program should handle the following commands:
- `ADD <title> <author>`: Adds a book with the given title and author to the library.
- `BORROW <title> <author>`: Borrows a book with the given title and author if it is available.
- `RETURN <title> <author>`: Returns a book with the given title and author if it was borrowed.
- `DISPLAY`: Displays all books in the library.

**Input:**
- The first line contains an integer `N` (1 ≤ N ≤ 100), the number of commands.
- The next `N` lines contain commands in the format described above.

**Output:**
- For each command, print the appropriate message as described in the operations.

**Sample Input:**
```
6
ADD 1984 GeorgeOrwell
ADD BraveNewWorld AldousHuxley
BORROW 1984 GeorgeOrwell
DISPLAY
RETURN 1984 GeorgeOrwell
DISPLAY
```

**Sample Output:**
```
The book '1984' by GeorgeOrwell has been added to the library.
The book 'BraveNewWorld' by AldousHuxley has been added to the library.
The book '1984' by GeorgeOrwell has been borrowed.
'1984' by GeorgeOrwell (Borrowed: Yes)
'BraveNewWorld' by AldousHuxley (Borrowed: No)
The book '1984' by GeorgeOrwell has been returned.
'1984' by GeorgeOrwell (Borrowed: No)
'BraveNewWorld' by AldousHuxley (Borrowed: No)
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author):
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.is_borrowed = False

    def borrow(self):
        """Borrow the book if it is not already borrowed."""
        if not self.is_borrowed:
            self.is_borrowed = True
            return f"The book '{self.title}' by {self.author} has been borrowed."
        else:
            return f"The book '{self.title}' by {self.author} is already borrowed."

    def return_book(self):
        """Return the book if it was borrowed."""
        if self.is_borrowed:
            self.is_borrowed = False
            return f"The book '{self.title}' by {self.author} has been returned."
        else:
            return f"The book '{self.title}' by {self.author} was not borrowed."

    def __str__(self):
        """Return a string representation of the book with its availability status."""
        return f"'{self.title}' by {self.author} (Borrowed: {'Yes' if self.is_borrowed else 'No'})"


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = []

    def add_book(self, title, author):
        """Add a new book to the library."""
        new_book = Book(title, author)
        self.books.append(new_book)
        return f"The book '{title}' by {author} has been added to the library."

    def borrow_book(self, title, author):
        """Borrow a book from the library if it is available."""
        for book in self.books:
            if book.title == title and book.author == author:
                return book.borrow()
        return f"The book '{title}' by {author} is not in the library."

    def return_book(self, title, author):
        """Return a book to the library if it was borrowed."""
        for book in self.books:
            if book.title == title and book.author == author:
                return book.return_book()
        return f"The book '{title}' by {author} is not in the library."

    def display_books(self):
        """Display all books in the library with their availability status."""
        if not self.books:
            return "The library is currently empty."
        else:
            return "\n".join(str(book) for book in self.books)


# Example usage:
library = Library()
commands = [
    "ADD 1984 GeorgeOrwell",
    "ADD BraveNewWorld AldousHuxley",
    "BORROW 1984 GeorgeOrwell",
    "DISPLAY",
    "RETURN 1984 GeorgeOrwell",
    "DISPLAY"
]

for command in commands:
    parts = command.split()
    if parts[0] == "ADD":
        print(library.add_book(parts[1], parts[2]))
    elif parts[0] == "BORROW":
        print(library.borrow_book(parts[1], parts[2]))
    elif parts[0] == "RETURN":
        print(library.return_book(parts[1], parts[2]))
    elif parts[0] == "DISPLAY":
        print(library.display_books())
```<#sep#>Implement a program to simulate a simple game of Hangman. The program should select a random word from a predefined list and prompt the user to guess letters of the word. The user has a maximum of 6 incorrect guesses. The game should display the current state of the word after each guess, with guessed letters revealed and unguessed letters hidden. If the user correctly guesses all the letters of the word, output a congratulatory message. If the user exhausts all their guesses without guessing the word, output the correct word and a message indicating the user has lost.

**Input:**
- The user will input a single letter at a time during the game.

**Output:**
- The program will display the current state of the word after each guess.
- After the game ends, the program will print either a congratulatory message or the correct word and a loss message.

**Sample Interaction:**
```
Welcome to Hangman!
Current word: _ _ _ _ _ _
Attempts remaining: 6
Guess a letter: P
Good guess!
Current word: P _ _ _ _ _
Attempts remaining: 6
Guess a letter: Y
Good guess!
Current word: P Y _ _ _ _
Attempts remaining: 6
Guess a letter: T
Good guess!
Current word: P Y T _ _ _
Attempts remaining: 6
Guess a letter: H
Good guess!
Current word: P Y T H _ _
Attempts remaining: 6
Guess a letter: O
Good guess!
Current word: P Y T H O _
Attempts remaining: 6
Guess a letter: N
Congratulations! You've guessed the word: P Y T H O N
```

```python
import random

def get_random_word(words):
    """
    Selects and returns a random word from the provided list, converted to uppercase.
    
    :param words: List of words to choose from.
    :return: A randomly selected word in uppercase.
    """
    return random.choice(words).upper()

def play_hangman():
    """
    Plays a game of Hangman where the user guesses letters of a randomly selected word.
    The user has a maximum of 6 incorrect guesses.
    """
    word_list = ['python', 'developer', 'hangman', 'challenge', 'project', 'algorithm']
    word_to_guess = get_random_word(word_list)
    guessed_letters = set()
    attempts_remaining = 6
    word_display = ['_'] * len(word_to_guess)

    print("Welcome to Hangman!")
    while attempts_remaining > 0 and '_' in word_display:
        print(f"Current word: {' '.join(word_display)}")
        print(f"Attempts remaining: {attempts_remaining}")
        guess = input("Guess a letter: ").upper()
        
        if len(guess) != 1 or not guess.isalpha():
            print("Please enter a single alphabetic character.")
            continue
        
        if guess in guessed_letters:
            print("You've already guessed that letter.")
            continue
        
        guessed_letters.add(guess)
        
        if guess in word_to_guess:
            for idx, letter in enumerate(word_to_guess):
                if letter == guess:
                    word_display[idx] = guess
            print("Good guess!")
        else:
            attempts_remaining -= 1
            print("Incorrect guess.")
    
    if '_' not in word_display:
        print(f"Congratulations! You've guessed the word: {' '.join(word_display)}")
    else:
        print(f"Sorry, you've run out of attempts. The word was: {word_to_guess}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    play_hangman()
```<#sep#>You are given a text file named `example.txt`. Your task is to write a Python script that reads this file, counts the frequency of each word while ignoring common stop words, and outputs the top 10 most frequent words along with their counts. The stop words to be ignored are: 'the', 'and', 'is', 'in', 'a', 'of', 'to', 'it', 'for', 'on', 'with', 'as', 'by', 'at', 'that', 'from', 'this', 'an', 'be', 'which', 'or', 'if', 'you', 'are', 'have', 'not', 'has', 'had', 'was', 'were', 'will', 'would', 'could', 'should', 'may', 'might', 'must', 'about', 'into', 'over', 'after', 'before', 'above', 'below', 'then', 'once', 'here', 'there', 'when', 'where', 'why', 'how', 'all', 'any', 'both', 'each', 'few', 'more', 'most', 'other', 'some', 'such', 'no', 'nor', 'not', 'only', 'own', 'same', 'so', 'than', 'too', 'very', 's', 't', 'can', 'will', 'just', 'don', 'should', 'now'.

**Sample Input (example.txt):**
```
The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. The dog was not amused.
```

**Sample Output:**
```
top 10 most frequent words:
the: 2
dog: 2
quick: 1
brown: 1
fox: 1
jumps: 1
over: 1
lazy: 1
was: 1
not: 1
```

```python
from collections import Counter
import re

def analyze_text_file(file_name):
    """
    Analyzes a text file to count the frequency of each word, ignoring common stop words,
    and outputs the top 10 most frequent words along with their counts.

    :param file_name: str, the name of the text file to analyze
    """
    # Define the stop words
    stop_words = {'the', 'and', 'is', 'in', 'a', 'of', 'to', 'it', 'for', 'on',
                  'with', 'as', 'by', 'at', 'that', 'from', 'this', 'an', 'be',
                  'which', 'or', 'if', 'you', 'are', 'have', 'not', 'has', 'had',
                  'was', 'were', 'will', 'would', 'could', 'should', 'may', 'might',
                  'must', 'about', 'into', 'over', 'after', 'before', 'above', 
                  'below', 'then', 'once', 'here', 'there', 'when', 'where', 'why', 
                  'how', 'all', 'any', 'both', 'each', 'few', 'more', 'most', 'other', 
                  'some', 'such', 'no', 'nor', 'not', 'only', 'own', 'same', 'so', 'than',
                  'too', 'very', 's', 't', 'can', 'will', 'just', 'don', 'should', 'now'}

    # Read the file
    with open(file_name, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as file:
        text = file.read().lower()  # Convert to lower case to count all variations of a word the same

    # Remove punctuation and split into words
    words = re.findall(r'\b\w+\b', text)

    # Remove stop words and count word frequencies
    filtered_words = [word for word in words if word not in stop_words]
    word_counts = Counter(filtered_words)

    # Get most common words
    common_words = word_counts.most_common(10)

    # Print results
    print("Top 10 most frequent words:")
    for word, count in common_words:
        print(f"{word}: {count}")

# Call the function with the filename
analyze_text_file('example.txt')
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Polynomial` class in Python that represents a polynomial of the form \( ax^n + bx^{n-1} + \ldots + c \). The class should support the following operations:
- Addition of two polynomials.
- Subtraction of two polynomials.
- Evaluation of the polynomial for a given value of \( x \).

Implement the `Polynomial` class with the following methods:
- `__init__(self, coefficients)`: Initializes the polynomial with a list of coefficients.
- `__add__(self, other)`: Overloads the addition operator to add two polynomials.
- `__sub__(self, other)`: Overloads the subtraction operator to subtract two polynomials.
- `evaluate(self, x)`: Evaluates the polynomial at a given value of \( x \).
- `__str__(self)`: Returns a string representation of the polynomial.

**Sample Input:**
```python
p1 = Polynomial([1, 2, 3])  # Represents 1x^2 + 2x + 3
p2 = Polynomial([3, 2, 1])  # Represents 3x^2 + 2x + 1
p3 = p1 + p2
p4 = p1 - p2
result = p1.evaluate(2)
```

**Sample Output:**
```
p1: 1x^2 + 2x + 3
p2: 3x^2 + 2x + 1
p1 + p2: 4x^2 + 4x + 4
p1 - p2: -2x^2 + 0x + 2
p1 evaluated at x=2: 11
```

```python
class Polynomial:
    def __init__(self, coefficients):
        """
        Initializes the polynomial with a list of coefficients.
        :param coefficients: List of coefficients, where the i-th element represents the coefficient of x^i.
        """
        self.coefficients = coefficients

    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Overloads the addition operator to add two polynomials.
        :param other: Another Polynomial object.
        :return: A new Polynomial object representing the sum of the two polynomials.
        """
        max_len = max(len(self.coefficients), len(other.coefficients))
        result = [0] * max_len
        for i in range(len(self.coefficients)):
            result[i] += self.coefficients[i]
        for i in range(len(other.coefficients)):
            result[i] += other.coefficients[i]
        return Polynomial(result)

    def __sub__(self, other):
        """
        Overloads the subtraction operator to subtract two polynomials.
        :param other: Another Polynomial object.
        :return: A new Polynomial object representing the difference of the two polynomials.
        """
        max_len = max(len(self.coefficients), len(other.coefficients))
        result = [0] * max_len
        for i in range(len(self.coefficients)):
            result[i] += self.coefficients[i]
        for i in range(len(other.coefficients)):
            result[i] -= other.coefficients[i]
        return Polynomial(result)

    def evaluate(self, x):
        """
        Evaluates the polynomial at a given value of x.
        :param x: The value at which to evaluate the polynomial.
        :return: The result of the polynomial evaluation.
        """
        result = 0
        for power, coeff in enumerate(reversed(self.coefficients)):
            result += coeff * (x ** power)
        return result

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Returns a string representation of the polynomial.
        :return: A string representing the polynomial.
        """
        terms = []
        for power, coeff in enumerate(reversed(self.coefficients)):
            if coeff != 0:
                term = f"{coeff}x^{power}" if power != 0 else f"{coeff}"
                terms.append(term)
        return " + ".join(terms)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple shopping cart system in Python. Your system should support the following operations:

1. **Add Item**: Add a specified quantity of an item at a given price to the cart.
2. **Remove Item**: Remove a specified quantity of an item from the cart. If the quantity to be removed is greater than or equal to the quantity in the cart, the item should be completely removed.
3. **Calculate Total Cost**: Compute the total cost of all items in the cart.
4. **Apply Discount**: Apply a discount percentage to the total cost and return the discounted total.
5. **Display Cart**: Print the contents of the cart, including the quantity and price of each item, and the total cost.

**Input Format**:
- The input will be a series of commands. Each command will be on a new line.
- Commands can be one of the following:
  - `ADD <item_name> <quantity> <price>`: Add the specified quantity of the item at the given price to the cart.
  - `REMOVE <item_name> <quantity>`: Remove the specified quantity of the item from the cart.
  - `TOTAL`: Calculate and print the total cost of the items in the cart.
  - `DISCOUNT <discount_percentage>`: Apply the specified discount percentage to the total cost and print the discounted total.
  - `DISPLAY`: Print the contents of the cart.

**Output Format**:
- For each `TOTAL`, `DISCOUNT`, and `DISPLAY` command, output the corresponding result as specified in the problem statement.

**Sample Input**:
```
ADD apple 10 0.5
ADD banana 5 0.3
DISPLAY
REMOVE apple 2
DISCOUNT 10
DISPLAY
```

**Sample Output**:
```
Shopping Cart:
10 of apple at $0.50 each ($5.00 total).
5 of banana at $0.30 each ($1.50 total).
Total cost: $6.50
Applied 10% discount. New total: $5.85.
Shopping Cart:
8 of apple at $0.50 each ($4.00 total).
5 of banana at $0.30 each ($1.50 total).
Total cost: $5.50
```

```python
class ShoppingCart:
    def __init__(self):
        self.items = []  # List of tuples where each tuple is (item_name, quantity, price)

    def add_item(self, item_name, quantity, price):
        """Add an item to the shopping cart."""
        self.items.append((item_name, quantity, price))
        print(f"Added {quantity} of {item_name} at ${price} each.")

    def remove_item(self, item_name, quantity):
        """Remove a specific quantity of an item from the shopping cart."""
        for i, (name, qty, price) in enumerate(self.items):
            if name == item_name:
                if qty > quantity:
                    self.items[i] = (name, qty - quantity, price)
                    print(f"Removed {quantity} of {item_name}.")
                elif qty == quantity:
                    self.items.pop(i)
                    print(f"Removed {item_name} completely.")
                else:
                    print(f"Cannot remove {quantity} of {item_name}: only {qty} in cart.")
                return
        print(f"Item {item_name} not found in cart.")

    def total_cost(self):
        """Calculate the total cost of the items in the cart."""
        total = 0
        for item in self.items:
            total += item[1] * item[2]
        return total

    def apply_discount(self, discount_percentage):
        """Apply a discount to the total cost."""
        if discount_percentage < 0 or discount_percentage > 100:
            print("Invalid discount percentage.")
            return

        discount_amount = (discount_percentage / 100) * self.total_cost()
        discounted_total = self.total_cost() - discount_amount
        print(f"Applied {discount_percentage}% discount. New total: ${discounted_total:.2f}.")
        return discounted_total

    def display_cart(self):
        """Display the contents of the shopping cart."""
        print("Shopping Cart:")
        if not self.items:
            print("No items in cart.")
            return
        for item in self.items:
            print(f"{item[1]} of {item[0]} at ${item[2]:.2f} each (${item[1] * item[2]:.2f} total).")
        print(f"Total cost: ${self.total_cost():.2f}")

# Example usage:
cart = ShoppingCart()
cart.add_item("apple", 10, 0.5)
cart.add_item("banana", 5, 0.3)
cart.display_cart()
cart.remove_item("apple", 2)
cart.apply_discount(10)
cart.display_cart()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BookManager` class in Python to manage a collection of books. The class should support the following operations:

1. **Add a Book**: Add a book to the collection. Each book is uniquely identified by its ISBN. If a book with the same ISBN already exists, it should not be added again.
2. **Remove a Book**: Remove a book from the collection using its ISBN. If the book does not exist, raise a `ValueError` with the message "Book with ISBN {isbn} not found."
3. **Search Books**: Search for books by a query string. The query should match either the title or the author of the book, ignoring case.
4. **Get All Books**: Retrieve a list of all books in the collection.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- **Input**: 
  - Add book: "1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935"
  - Add book: "To Kill a Mockingbird", "Harper Lee", "9780060935467"
  - Search books: "orwell"
  - Remove book: "9780451524935"
  - Get all books

- **Output**:
  - Search result: [{'title': '1984', 'author': 'George Orwell', 'isbn': '9780451524935'}]
  - All books: [{'title': 'To Kill a Mockingbird', 'author': 'Harper Lee', 'isbn': '9780060935467'}]

```python
class BookManager:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty list to store books."""
        self.books = []

    def add_book(self, title, author, isbn):
        """
        Add a book to the collection if it does not already exist.
        
        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param isbn: The ISBN of the book.
        """
        if any(book['isbn'] == isbn for book in self.books):
            return  # Book already exists, do not add again
        
        new_book = {'title': title, 'author': author, 'isbn': isbn}
        self.books.append(new_book)

    def remove_book(self, isbn):
        """
        Remove a book from the collection by its ISBN.
        
        :param isbn: The ISBN of the book to remove.
        :raises ValueError: If the book with the given ISBN is not found.
        """
        for index, book in enumerate(self.books):
            if book['isbn'] == isbn:
                del self.books[index]
                return
        
        raise ValueError(f"Book with ISBN {isbn} not found.")

    def search_books(self, query):
        """
        Search for books by a query string in the title or author.
        
        :param query: The search query string.
        :return: A list of books matching the query.
        """
        return [book for book in self.books if query.lower() in book['title'].lower() or query.lower() in book['author'].lower()]

    def get_all_books(self):
        """
        Retrieve a list of all books in the collection.
        
        :return: A list of all books.
        """
        return self.books
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a function `optimize_function(f, bounds)` that utilizes a genetic algorithm to find the minimum value of a given mathematical function `f(x)`. The `bounds` parameter is a tuple representing the inclusive range `[low, high]` within which the variable `x` can vary. Your function should return the value of `x` that minimizes `f(x)`. Use the `deap` library to implement the genetic algorithm. The genetic algorithm should be configured with a population size of 100, a maximum of 50 generations, a crossover probability of 0.7, and a mutation probability of 0.2. The mutation should use a Gaussian distribution with a mean of 0 and a standard deviation of 10% of the range of `bounds`.

**Sample Input:**
```python
def sample_function(x):
    return (x - 3) ** 2 + 4

bounds = (0, 10)
```

**Sample Output:**
```
2.9999999999999996
```

```python
from deap import base, creator, tools, algorithms
import random

def optimize_function(f, bounds):
    """
    Uses a genetic algorithm to find the minimum of a given mathematical function f(x) within specified bounds.

    Parameters:
    f (function): The mathematical function to minimize.
    bounds (tuple): A tuple (low, high) representing the inclusive range for x.

    Returns:
    float: The value of x that minimizes f(x).
    """
    # Constants for genetic algorithm
    POP_SIZE = 100  # Population size
    MAX_GEN = 50    # Number of generations
    CX_PB = 0.7     # Crossover probability
    MUT_PB = 0.2    # Mutation probability

    # Define the problem as a minimization problem
    creator.create("FitnessMin", base.Fitness, weights=(-1.0,))
    creator.create("Individual", list, fitness=creator.FitnessMin)

    # Define the toolbox
    toolbox = base.Toolbox()
    toolbox.register("attr_float", random.uniform, bounds[0], bounds[1])
    toolbox.register("individual", tools.initRepeat, creator.Individual, toolbox.attr_float, 1)
    toolbox.register("population", tools.initRepeat, list, toolbox.individual)
    toolbox.register("evaluate", f)
    toolbox.register("mate", tools.cxBlend, alpha=0.5)
    toolbox.register("mutate", tools.mutGaussian, mu=0, sigma=(bounds[1]-bounds[0])*0.1, indpb=1)
    toolbox.register("select", tools.selTournament, tournsize=3)

    # Initialize the population
    population = toolbox.population(n=POP_SIZE)

    # Run the genetic algorithm
    best_individual = None
    for gen in range(MAX_GEN):
        offspring = algorithms.varAnd(population, toolbox, cxpb=CX_PB, mutpb=MUT_PB)
        fits = toolbox.map(toolbox.evaluate, offspring)
        for fit, ind in zip(fits, offspring):
            ind.fitness.values = (fit,)
        population = toolbox.select(offspring, k=POP_SIZE)

        # Track the best individual
        best_softmax = tools.selBest(population, k=1)[0]
        if best_individual is None or best_softmax.fitness.values[0] < best_individual.fitness.values[0]:
            best_individual = best_softmax

    return best_individual[0]
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file with a header row and multiple data rows. Your task is to write a Python program that reads this CSV file and computes the average of a specified column. The program should accept two command-line arguments: the path to the CSV file and the name of the column for which the average should be calculated. If the specified column does not exist, the program should output an error message and terminate.

**Input:**
- The first argument is the path to the CSV file.
- The second argument is the name of the column.

**Output:**
- The average of the specified column, formatted as "The average of column 'column_name' is X", where X is the calculated average.
- If the column does not exist, output "Error: Column 'column_name' does not exist in the CSV file."

**Sample Input:**
```
data.csv
Age
```

**Sample Output:**
```
The average of column 'Age' is 30.5
```

```python
import pandas as pd
import sys
import argparse

def calculate_average(csv_file, column_name):
    """
    Calculate the average of a specified column in a CSV file.

    Parameters:
    csv_file (str): Path to the CSV file.
    column_name (str): Name of the column to calculate the average.

    Returns:
    None: Outputs the average of the specified column or an error message.
    """
    # Read the CSV file into a DataFrame
    df = pd.read_csv(csv_file)
    
    # Check if the specified column exists in the DataFrame
    if column_name not in df.columns:
        print(f"Error: Column '{column_name}' does not exist in the CSV file.")
        sys.exit(1)
        
    # Calculate the average of the specified column
    column_average = df[column_name].mean()
